- **C — Xylem (Correct):** Xylem is a complex vascular tissue (comprising tracheids, vessels, xylem parenchyma, and fibres) that conducts water and dissolved mineral salts unidirectionally from roots to aerial parts.
- **A — Phloem:** Vascular tissue that translocates synthesized sugars (sucrose) bidirectionally from source leaves to sinks.
- **B — Parenchyma:** Living ground tissue involved in photosynthesis, storage, and wound healing.
- **D — Collenchyma:** Living mechanical tissue with unevenly thickened cell walls providing flexible support to young stems and petioles.
- **Exam Extra:** Transpiration pull and cohesion-tension of water molecules drive upward xylem sap movement in tall trees.

### Q481 · Plant Physiology & Biological Branches

Which vascular tissue in plants is responsible for the translocation of synthesized food (sucrose) from leaves to other parts?

A. Xylem
B. Sclerenchyma
C. Cambium
D. Phloem

**Answer:** D. Phloem
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — Phloem (Correct):** Phloem (comprising sieve tubes, companion cells, phloem parenchyma, and fibres) translocates organic food (mainly sucrose) bidirectionally from photosynthetic leaves to growing tissues and storage organs.
- **A — Xylem:** Conducts water and mineral ions upward from roots to shoots.
- **B — Sclerenchyma:** Dead mechanical tissue with heavily lignified walls providing rigidity (e.g., coconut husk fibres, seed coats).
- **C — Cambium:** Lateral meristematic layer that divides to produce secondary xylem and secondary phloem during stem thickening.
- **Exam Extra:** Unlike dead xylem vessels and tracheids, sieve tube elements and companion cells of phloem are living at maturity.

### Q482 · Plant Physiology & Biological Branches

Through which tiny epidermal pores on leaves do plants primarily carry out transpiration and gaseous exchange?

A. Stomata
B. Lenticels
C. Hydathodes
D. Plasmodesmata

**Answer:** A. Stomata
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — Stomata (Correct):** Stomata are microscopic epidermal pores flanked by two guard cells that regulate carbon dioxide uptake for photosynthesis, oxygen release, and water vapour loss (transpiration).
- **B — Lenticels:** Lens-shaped openings in woody bark that permit gas exchange in stems and roots.
- **C — Hydathodes:** Specialized pores at leaf margins or tips through which liquid water is exuded during guttation.
- **D — Plasmodesmata:** Microscopic cytoplasmic channels connecting adjacent plant cell walls for intercellular transport.
- **Exam Extra:** In most dicot leaves, stomata are more numerous on the lower (abaxial) surface to minimize excessive water loss.

### Q483 · Plant Physiology & Biological Branches

Which gaseous plant hormone promotes the ripening of fruits and senescence of leaves?

A. Auxin
B. Ethylene
C. Gibberellin
D. Cytokinin

**Answer:** B. Ethylene
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — Ethylene (Correct):** Ethylene (C2H4) is the only gaseous plant hormone; it accelerates fruit ripening, triggers the climacteric rise in respiration, and promotes leaf and flower senescence and abscission.
- **A — Auxin:** Indole-3-acetic acid (IAA); promotes cell elongation, apical dominance, and phototropism.
- **C — Gibberellin:** Promotes stem elongation (bolting), breaks seed dormancy, and increases fruit size.
- **D — Cytokinin:** Promotes cell division (cytokinesis), delays leaf senescence, and overcomes apical dominance.
- **Exam Extra:** Calcium carbide or ethephon releases acetylene/ethylene gas and is associated with artificial fruit ripening.

### Q484 · Plant Physiology & Biological Branches

Which plant hormone is known as the 'stress hormone' because it induces stomatal closure and seed dormancy during drought?

A. Auxin
B. Cytokinin
C. Abscisic acid
D. Gibberellin

**Answer:** C. Abscisic acid
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — Abscisic acid (Correct):** Abscisic acid (ABA) accumulates during drought or salinity stress, triggering rapid stomatal closure to conserve water and maintaining seed dormancy until favourable conditions return.
- **A — Auxin:** Growth-promoting hormone regulating cell elongation, root initiation, and tropic movements.
- **B — Cytokinin:** Growth-promoting hormone stimulating cell division and delaying leaf ageing.
- **D — Gibberellin:** Growth-promoting hormone stimulating internode elongation and seed germination.
- **Exam Extra:** Abscisic acid acts antagonistically to gibberellins in regulating seed germination.

### Q485 · Plant Physiology & Biological Branches

What is the branch of biology concerned with the scientific study of fungi?

A. Phycology
B. Bryology
C. Entomology
D. Mycology

**Answer:** D. Mycology
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — Mycology (Correct):** Mycology is the branch of biology devoted to the taxonomy, genetics, biochemistry, and ecological roles of fungi (including mushrooms, yeasts, and moulds).
- **A — Phycology:** Branch of botany concerned with the scientific study of algae (also called algology).
- **B — Bryology:** Branch of botany dealing with bryophytes (mosses, liverworts, and hornworts).
- **C — Entomology:** Branch of zoology devoted to the scientific study of insects.
- **Exam Extra:** P.A. Micheli is often called the father of mycology for his 1729 work Nova Plantarum Genera.

---

# Section III: Static GK & Culture

## Chapter 8: Art, Culture, Heritage & Festivals

### Q486 · Classical Dances & Awardee Exponents

Bharatanatyam is classically associated with which Indian state?

A. Andhra Pradesh
B. Tamil Nadu
C. Kerala
D. Karnataka

**Answer:** B. Tamil Nadu
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — Tamil Nadu (Correct):** Bharatanatyam originated in Tamil Nadu, evolving from the ancient temple dance tradition of Sadir (Dasiattam) codified in Bharata Muni's Natyashastra.
- **A — Andhra Pradesh:** Classical dance form is Kuchipudi, originating in the village of Kuchelapuram.
- **C — Kerala:** Home to two classical dance forms—Kathakali and Mohiniyattam.
- **D — Karnataka:** Famous for the traditional dance-drama Yakshagana rather than a standalone Sangeet Natak Akademi classical dance.
- **Exam Extra:** E. Krishna Iyer and Rukmini Devi Arundale played pivotal roles in reviving Bharatanatyam and establishing it on the modern concert stage in the 1930s.

### Q487 · Classical Dances & Awardee Exponents

Kathakali is a classical dance-drama tradition belonging to which state?

A. Tamil Nadu
B. Karnataka
C. Kerala
D. Andhra Pradesh

**Answer:** C. Kerala
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — Kerala (Correct):** Kathakali is a stylized classical dance-drama tradition of Kerala known for elaborate makeup (vesham), towering headgear, and expressive eye and hand gestures (mudras).
- **A — Tamil Nadu:** Classical dance is Bharatanatyam, and folk theatre includes Terukoothu.
- **B — Karnataka:** Traditional folk dance-drama is Yakshagana, and classical music follows the Carnatic tradition.
- **D — Andhra Pradesh:** Classical dance-drama tradition is Kuchipudi, founded by Siddhendra Yogi.
- **Exam Extra:** Kathakali stories are traditionally drawn from the Ramayana, Mahabharata, and Puranas, and accompanied by percussion instruments like the chenda and maddalam.

### Q488 · Classical Dances & Awardee Exponents

Which classical dance form of India originated in Assam and was introduced by the 15th-century Vaishnava saint Srimanta Sankardev?

A. Sattriya
B. Manipuri
C. Odissi
D. Kuchipudi

**Answer:** A. Sattriya
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — Sattriya (Correct):** Sattriya originated in the Vaishnava monasteries (Sattras) of Assam, founded in the 15th century by saint-reformer Srimanta Sankardev as a medium for propagating Bhakti.
- **B — Manipuri:** Classical dance form of Manipur centered on the Raslila of Radha and Krishna.
- **C — Odissi:** Classical dance form of Odisha known for the tribhanga (three-bend) posture.
- **D — Kuchipudi:** Classical dance form of Andhra Pradesh blending pure dance (nritta) and expressive drama (natya).
- **Exam Extra:** Sattriya was recognized as the eighth classical dance form of India by the Sangeet Natak Akademi in the year 2000.

### Q489 · Classical Dances & Awardee Exponents

Mohiniyattam, characterized by gentle swaying movements and white-and-gold kasavu attire, is a classical dance of which state?

A. Tamil Nadu
B. Odisha
C. Andhra Pradesh
D. Kerala

**Answer:** D. Kerala
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — Kerala (Correct):** Mohiniyattam, literally the 'dance of the enchantress,' is a graceful lasya-style classical solo dance of Kerala performed in traditional white-and-gold kasavu attire.
- **A — Tamil Nadu:** Classical dance is Bharatanatyam, characterized by geometric lines and rhythmic footwork.
- **B — Odisha:** Classical dance is Odissi, inspired by sculptures in temples like Konark and Jagannath Puri.
- **C — Andhra Pradesh:** Classical dance is Kuchipudi, famous for sequences like dancing on the rim of a brass plate (tarangam).
- **Exam Extra:** Maharaja Swathi Thirunal of Travancore and poet Vallathol Narayana Menon were major patrons who helped revive Mohiniyattam in the 19th and 20th centuries.

### Q490 · Classical Dances & Awardee Exponents

Kuchipudi derives its name from a village in the Krishna district of which state?

A. Telangana
B. Andhra Pradesh
C. Karnataka
D. Tamil Nadu

**Answer:** B. Andhra Pradesh
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — Andhra Pradesh (Correct):** Kuchipudi takes its name from Kuchelapuram (Kuchipudi) village in Krishna district, Andhra Pradesh, where Siddhendra Yogi formalized it as a Bhagavata Mela dance-drama.
- **A — Telangana:** Associated with the Perini Shivatandavam (warrior dance of the Kakatiya period) rather than Kuchipudi's origin.
- **C — Karnataka:** Known for Yakshagana, Dollu Kunitha, and Mysore style Bharatanatyam traditions.
- **D — Tamil Nadu:** Birthplace of Bharatanatyam, Bhagavata Mela Nataka of Melattur, and Karakattam.
- **Exam Extra:** A signature feature of Kuchipudi is the Tarangam, in which the dancer balances on the edges of a brass plate while keeping a pot of water on the head.

### Q491 · Classical Dances & Awardee Exponents

Pandit Birju Maharaj, a Padma Vibhushan awardee of the Lucknow gharana, was a legendary exponent of which classical dance?

A. Odissi
B. Manipuri
C. Kathak
D. Bharatanatyam

**Answer:** C. Kathak
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — Kathak (Correct):** Pandit Birju Maharaj (Brijmohan Nath Mishra) was the foremost doyen of the Kalka-Bindadin (Lucknow) gharana of Kathak and received the Padma Vibhushan in 1986.
- **A — Odissi:** Associated with maestros like Guru Kelucharan Mohapatra, Pankaj Charan Das, and Sanjukta Panigrahi.
- **B — Manipuri:** Associated with exponents like Guru Bipin Singh, Jhaveri sisters, and Darshana Jhaveri.
- **D — Bharatanatyam:** Associated with Rukmini Devi Arundale, Balasaraswati, and Padma Subrahmanyam.
- **Exam Extra:** Kathak is the only classical dance of northern India among the eight Sangeet Natak Akademi dances and developed across three major gharanas: Lucknow, Jaipur, and Banaras.

### Q492 · Classical Dances & Awardee Exponents

Who is credited with reviving Bharatanatyam and founding the Kalakshetra Foundation in Chennai in 1936?

A. Rukmini Devi Arundale
B. Yamini Krishnamurthy
C. Mrinalini Sarabhai
D. T. Balasaraswati

**Answer:** A. Rukmini Devi Arundale
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — Rukmini Devi Arundale (Correct):** Rukmini Devi Arundale founded Kalakshetra in Adyar, Chennai in 1936, transforming Bharatanatyam into a respected classical concert art and designing its modern costumes and stage aesthetics.
- **B — Yamini Krishnamurthy:** Renowned Bharatanatyam and Kuchipudi dancer who founded the Yamini School of Dance in Delhi.
- **C — Mrinalini Sarabhai:** Celebrated Bharatanatyam and Kathakali exponent who founded the Darpana Academy of Performing Arts in Ahmedabad in 1949.
- **D — T. Balasaraswati:** Legendary hereditary exponent of the Thanjavur Sadir tradition who received the Padma Vibhushan in 1977.
- **Exam Extra:** Rukmini Devi Arundale was also the first woman in Indian history to be nominated to the Rajya Sabha (1952) and was awarded the Padma Bhushan in 1956.

### Q493 · Classical Dances & Awardee Exponents

Guru Kelucharan Mohapatra, the first person from Odisha to receive the Padma Vibhushan, was a pioneering guru of which classical dance?

A. Kuchipudi
B. Manipuri
C. Kathakali
D. Odissi

**Answer:** D. Odissi
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — Odissi (Correct):** Guru Kelucharan Mohapatra revived Odissi in the 20th century from the Gotipua and Mahari traditions and became the first person from Odisha to receive the Padma Vibhushan (2000).
- **A — Kuchipudi:** Systematized and popularized in modern times by Guru Vempati Chinna Satyam and Vedantam Lakshminarayana Sastry.
- **B — Manipuri:** Modernized for the proscenium stage by Guru Bipin Singh and Guru Amubi Singh.
- **C — Kathakali:** Revived in the 20th century by Vallathol Narayana Menon at Kerala Kalamandalam with gurus like Kunju Kurup.
- **Exam Extra:** Odissi is distinguished by two foundational postures: Chowka (a square, masculine stance) and Tribhanga (a graceful S-shaped three-bend feminine posture).

### Q494 · Classical Dances & Awardee Exponents

Which classical dancer is renowned for both Bharatanatyam and Kuchipudi and served as the Asthana Narthaki of Tirumala Tirupati Devasthanam?

A. Sonal Mansingh
B. Yamini Krishnamurthy
C. Sitara Devi
D. Shovana Narayan

**Answer:** B. Yamini Krishnamurthy
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — Yamini Krishnamurthy (Correct):** Yamini Krishnamurthy mastered both Bharatanatyam and Kuchipudi, earning the Padma Shri (1968), Padma Bhushan (2001), and Padma Vibhushan (2016), and served as Asthana Narthaki of Tirumala Tirupati Devasthanam.
- **A — Sonal Mansingh:** Celebrated exponent of Bharatanatyam and Odissi, awarded the Padma Vibhushan in 2003.
- **C — Sitara Devi:** Legendary Kathak virtuoso of the Banaras gharana, nicknamed 'Nritya Samragni' by Rabindranath Tagore.
- **D — Shovana Narayan:** Renowned Kathak exponent and Padma Shri awardee who also served as a senior IA&AS officer.
- **Exam Extra:** Her autobiography, titled 'A Passion for Dance,' chronicles her journey across both South Indian classical traditions.

### Q495 · Classical Dances & Awardee Exponents

Guru Bipin Singh, often called the 'Father of Manipuri Dance' in modern times, received the Sangeet Natak Akademi Award for which dance form?

A. Sattriya
B. Odissi
C. Manipuri
D. Kathak

**Answer:** C. Manipuri
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — Manipuri (Correct):** Guru Bipin Singh was a master teacher, choreographer, and researcher of Manipuri dance who trained the famous Jhaveri sisters and founded the Govindji Nartanalaya in Imphal.
- **A — Sattriya:** Propagated in modern times by scholars and gurus such as Jatin Goswami and Ghanakanta Bora.
- **B — Odissi:** Revived by Adi Guru Pankaj Charan Das, Debaprasad Das, and Kelucharan Mohapatra.
- **D — Kathak:** Associated with stalwarts such as Shambhu Maharaj, Lachhu Maharaj, and Birju Maharaj.
- **Exam Extra:** Manipuri dance is known for its restrained, circular movements and the iconic stiff barrel-shaped skirt called the 'Kumil' worn in Raslila performances.

### Q496 · Classical Dances & Awardee Exponents

Kalamandalam Kalyanikutty Amma is famously known as the 'Mother of' which Indian classical dance form?

A. Mohiniyattam
B. Kathakali
C. Bharatanatyam
D. Kuchipudi

**Answer:** A. Mohiniyattam
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — Mohiniyattam (Correct):** Kalamandalam Kalyanikutty Amma is revered as the 'Mother of Mohiniyattam' for rescuing the art from near extinction through research, new compositions, and teaching at Kerala Kalamandalam.
- **B — Kathakali:** Traditionally male-dominated dance-drama associated with gurus like Kalamandalam Ramankutty Nair and Gopi.
- **C — Bharatanatyam:** Key 20th-century matriarchs include Rukmini Devi Arundale and T. Balasaraswati.
- **D — Kuchipudi:** Traditionally revived by male Bhagavatulu gurus and later popularized by female exponents like Yamini Krishnamurthy and Swapnasundari.
- **Exam Extra:** She authored the authoritative Malayalam treatise 'Mohiniyattam: Charitravum Aattaprakaravum' documenting its history and technique.

### Q497 · Classical Dances & Awardee Exponents

Vempati Chinna Satyam was a Padma Bhushan awardee who popularized and systematized which classical dance tradition worldwide?

A. Odissi
B. Bharatanatyam
C. Kathakali
D. Kuchipudi

**Answer:** D. Kuchipudi
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — Kuchipudi (Correct):** Vempati Chinna Satyam codified Kuchipudi's solo and dance-drama repertoire, founded the Kuchipudi Art Academy in Madras (1963), and received the Padma Bhushan in 1998.
- **A — Odissi:** Modern codification was led by the Jayantika group of gurus including Kelucharan Mohapatra and Mayadhar Raut.
- **B — Bharatanatyam:** Codified in the 19th century by the Tanjore Quartet (Chinnayya, Ponnayya, Sivanandam, and Vadivelu).
- **C — Kathakali:** Codified in its modern form through Kerala Kalamandalam established in 1930.
- **Exam Extra:** He choreographed over 180 solo items and 15 full-length dance-dramas, including 'Srinivasa Kalyanam' and 'Ksheera Sagara Madhanam.'

### Q498 · Classical Dances & Awardee Exponents

Which Kathak virtuoso of the Banaras gharana was titled 'Nritya Samragni' and declined the Padma Bhushan stating she deserved the Bharat Ratna?

A. Kumudini Lakhia
B. Sitara Devi
C. Maya Rao
D. Saswati Sen

**Answer:** B. Sitara Devi
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — Sitara Devi (Correct):** Sitara Devi of the Banaras gharana was hailed by Rabindranath Tagore as 'Nritya Samragni' (Empress of Dance) and famously refused the Padma Bhushan in 2002, saying she deserved the Bharat Ratna.
- **A — Kumudini Lakhia:** Pioneer of contemporary Kathak choreography who founded the Kadamb Centre for Dance in Ahmedabad.
- **C — Maya Rao:** Noted Kathak choreographer and Natya Institute of Kathak and Choreography founder from Karnataka.
- **D — Saswati Sen:** Senior disciple of Pandit Birju Maharaj and prominent exponent of the Lucknow gharana.
- **Exam Extra:** Unlike the Jaipur gharana (noted for brisk footwork) and Lucknow gharana (noted for expressive abhinaya), the Banaras gharana of Kathak emphasizes asymmetrical stances and Natwari bol rhythms.

### Q499 · Classical Dances & Awardee Exponents

Sonal Mansingh, a Padma Vibhushan recipient and former Rajya Sabha MP, is most celebrated as an exponent of which pair of classical dances?

A. Kathak and Manipuri
B. Kathakali and Mohiniyattam
C. Bharatanatyam and Odissi
D. Kuchipudi and Sattriya

**Answer:** C. Bharatanatyam and Odissi
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — Bharatanatyam and Odissi (Correct):** Sonal Mansingh is celebrated globally for her mastery of both Bharatanatyam and Odissi, receiving the Padma Bhushan (1992) and becoming the youngest dancer to win the Padma Vibhushan (2003).
- **A — Kathak and Manipuri:** Primarily associated with separate northern and northeastern traditions (e.g., Birju Maharaj for Kathak, Jhaveri sisters for Manipuri).
- **B — Kathakali and Mohiniyattam:** Kerala's twin classical dances, famously performed together by artists like Mrinalini Sarabhai (with Bharatanatyam) or Kalamandalam Gopi/Kalyanikutty Amma individually.
- **D — Kuchipudi and Sattriya:** Distinct traditions of Andhra Pradesh and Assam respectively, rarely paired as a primary dual specialization.
- **Exam Extra:** She founded the Centre for Indian Classical Dances (CICD) in New Delhi in 1977 and was nominated to the Rajya Sabha in 2018.

### Q500 · State Folk & Tribal Dances, Theatre & Martial Arts

Kalbelia, a folk song and dance tradition inscribed on UNESCO's Intangible Cultural Heritage list in 2010, belongs to which state?

A. Rajasthan
B. Gujarat
C. Haryana
D. Madhya Pradesh

**Answer:** A. Rajasthan
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — Rajasthan (Correct):** Kalbelia is a traditional folk song and dance of the snake-charmer Kalbelia community of Rajasthan, inscribed on UNESCO's Intangible Cultural Heritage list in 2010.
- **B — Gujarat:** Famous for Garba, Dandiya Raas, Tippani, and Bhavai folk traditions.
- **C — Haryana:** Known for Phag, Daph, Dhamal, and Khoria folk dances.
- **D — Madhya Pradesh:** Known for Matki, Jawara, Tertali, and Rai folk dances.
- **Exam Extra:** Gulabo Sapera, a Padma Shri awardee, played a leading role in bringing the Kalbelia dance to international stages.

### Q501 · State Folk & Tribal Dances, Theatre & Martial Arts

Cheraw, popularly known as the 'Bamboo Dance,' is a traditional folk dance of which northeastern state?

A. Nagaland
B. Manipur
C. Tripura
D. Mizoram

**Answer:** D. Mizoram
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — Mizoram (Correct):** Cheraw, famously known as the Bamboo Dance, is the oldest and most iconic folk dance of Mizoram, where dancers step gracefully between rhythmic clapping bamboo staves.
- **A — Nagaland:** Famous for war and harvest dances such as Chang Lo (Sua Lua) and Zeliang dance.
- **B — Manipur:** Known for Thang-Ta, Pung Cholom, Dhol Cholom, and Lai Haraoba dances.
- **C — Tripura:** Famous for Hojagiri dance (performed by the Reang/Bru community balancing on earthen pitchers) and Garia dance.
- **Exam Extra:** Other popular folk dances of Mizoram include Khuallam, Chheihlam, and Solakia.

### Q502 · State Folk & Tribal Dances, Theatre & Martial Arts

Rauf (Rouf) is a traditional folk dance performed by women during harvest and festive occasions in which region?

A. Himachal Pradesh
B. Jammu and Kashmir
C. Uttarakhand
D. Ladakh

**Answer:** B. Jammu and Kashmir
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — Jammu and Kashmir (Correct):** Rauf (or Rouf) is a traditional folk dance of the Kashmir Valley in Jammu and Kashmir, performed by women standing in two rows facing each other during spring and Eid.
- **A — Himachal Pradesh:** Famous for Nati, Chham, Rakshasa (Demon) dance, and Dangi.
- **C — Uttarakhand:** Known for Chholiya (sword dance of Kumaon), Barada Nati, and Jhora.
- **D — Ladakh:** Known for Jabro, Shondol (the royal dance of Ladakh), and monastic Cham mask dances.
- **Exam Extra:** Another prominent folk dance of the Kashmir region is Dumhal, performed exclusively by men of the Wattal tribe.

### Q503 · State Folk & Tribal Dances, Theatre & Martial Arts

Chhau, a semi-classical martial folk dance inscribed on UNESCO's ICH list, is NOT traditionally associated with which of the following states?

A. Odisha
B. Jharkhand
C. Chhattisgarh
D. West Bengal

**Answer:** C. Chhattisgarh
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — Chhattisgarh (Correct):** Chhau has three recognized regional styles—Seraikella (Jharkhand), Purulia (West Bengal), and Mayurbhanj (Odisha)—so Chhattisgarh is not one of its traditional home states.
- **A — Odisha:** Home to Mayurbhanj Chhau, a maskless martial dance tradition of northern Odisha.
- **B — Jharkhand:** Home to Seraikella Chhau, noted for expressive masks and royal patronage.
- **D — West Bengal:** Home to Purulia Chhau, famous for vibrant masks and acrobatic leaps.
- **Exam Extra:** While Seraikella and Purulia Chhau use elaborate masks, Mayurbhanj Chhau is performed without masks; Chhau was inscribed by UNESCO in 2010.

### Q504 · State Folk & Tribal Dances, Theatre & Martial Arts

Lavani, a lively folk music and dance genre performed to the rhythm of the dholki, is a signature art form of which state?

A. Gujarat
B. Maharashtra
C. Goa
D. Karnataka

**Answer:** B. Maharashtra
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — Maharashtra (Correct):** Lavani is a popular traditional music and dance form of Maharashtra, performed briskly to the beat of the dholki by women wearing nine-yard Nauvari sarees.
- **A — Gujarat:** Famous for Garba and Dandiya Raas performed during Navratri.
- **C — Goa:** Known for Fugdi, Dhalo, Dekhni, and Kunbi folk dances.
- **D — Karnataka:** Known for Dollu Kunitha, Veeragase, Kamsale, and Yakshagana.
- **Exam Extra:** Lavani is closely linked with Tamasha, the traditional Marathi folk theatre, and has two main forms: Nirguni Lavani (philosophical) and Shringari Lavani (romantic).

### Q505 · State Folk & Tribal Dances, Theatre & Martial Arts

Yakshagana, a traditional theatre art combining dance, music, dialogue, and ornate headgear, belongs to which state?

A. Karnataka
B. Andhra Pradesh
C. Tamil Nadu
D. Kerala

**Answer:** A. Karnataka
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — Karnataka (Correct):** Yakshagana is a traditional theatre form of coastal and Malenadu Karnataka combining dance, music, extempore dialogue, and vibrant costumes with ornate headgear (pagade/mundasu).
- **B — Andhra Pradesh:** Known for Burra Katha, Tholu Bommalata (shadow puppetry), and Kuchipudi.
- **C — Tamil Nadu:** Known for Terukoothu, Villu Paatu, and Bommalattam puppetry.
- **D — Kerala:** Known for Kathakali, Kutiyattam, Theyyam, and Chakyar Koothu.
- **Exam Extra:** Its two major regional variations are Tenkuthittu (southern style) and Badaguthittu (northern style).

### Q506 · State Folk & Tribal Dances, Theatre & Martial Arts

Padma Vibhushan awardee Teejan Bai is a globally acclaimed exponent of Pandavani, a folk narrative art of which state?

A. Jharkhand
B. Madhya Pradesh
C. Odisha
D. Chhattisgarh

**Answer:** D. Chhattisgarh
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — Chhattisgarh (Correct):** Pandavani is a folk ballad storytelling tradition of Chhattisgarh narrating tales of the Pandavas from the Mahabharata; Teejan Bai popularized its energetic Kapalik style globally and received the Padma Vibhushan in 2019.
- **A — Jharkhand:** Famous for Jhumair, Paika, and Chhau folk traditions.
- **B — Madhya Pradesh:** Known for Alha ballad singing, Rai, and Bhagoria folk arts.
- **C — Odisha:** Known for Daskathia and Pala narrative folk traditions.
- **Exam Extra:** Pandavani has two distinct styles: Vedamati (performed sitting calmly) and Kapalik (performed standing with dramatic enactments using a tambura).

### Q507 · State Folk & Tribal Dances, Theatre & Martial Arts

Thang-Ta, an ancient martial art combining sword (thang) and spear (ta), originates from which state?

A. Assam
B. Manipur
C. Arunachal Pradesh
D. Sikkim

**Answer:** B. Manipur
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — Manipur (Correct):** Thang-Ta (known traditionally as Huyen Langlon) is an armed martial art of Manipur using the sword (thang) and spear (ta), integrated with rhythmic breathing and ritual dance.
- **A — Assam:** Known for traditional wrestling and Bihu/Bagurumba dances rather than Thang-Ta.
- **C — Arunachal Pradesh:** Known for Aji Lamu, Ponung, and Buiya folk dances.
- **D — Sikkim:** Known for Singhi Chham (Snow Lion dance) and Yak Chham.
- **Exam Extra:** Unarmed hand-to-hand combat in the same Manipuri martial tradition is called Sarit Sarak.

### Q508 · State Folk & Tribal Dances, Theatre & Martial Arts

Kalaripayattu, widely regarded as one of the oldest surviving martial arts in the world, originated in which state?

A. Tamil Nadu
B. Karnataka
C. Kerala
D. Maharashtra

**Answer:** C. Kerala
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — Kerala (Correct):** Kalaripayattu is an ancient Indian martial art from Kerala, practiced in traditional training arenas called 'kalari' and featuring weapons like urumi (flexible sword),ottu, and kathi.
- **A — Tamil Nadu:** Home to the ancient Tamil martial arts Silambam and Varma Kalai.
- **B — Karnataka:** Associated with traditional wrestling (Garadi Mane) and Veeragase martial dance.
- **D — Maharashtra:** Associated with Mardani Khel, an armed martial art linked to the Maratha era.
- **Exam Extra:** Kalaripayattu comprises two main traditions: Vadakkan (Northern style, emphasizing weapons and jumps) and Thekkan (Southern style, emphasizing bare-hand strikes and marmam pressure points).

### Q509 · State Folk & Tribal Dances, Theatre & Martial Arts

Gatka, a traditional martial art practiced with wooden sticks (soti) and shields, is primarily associated with which state?

A. Punjab
B. Haryana
C. Rajasthan
D. Uttar Pradesh

**Answer:** A. Punjab
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — Punjab (Correct):** Gatka is a traditional Sikh martial art originating in Punjab, practiced with wooden sticks (soti) and shields (farri) in mock combat and religious processions.
- **B — Haryana:** Known for traditional Akhara wrestling (Kushti) and folk dances like Dhamal.
- **C — Rajasthan:** Known for Dandiya/Gair stick dances and Rajput swordsmanship traditions.
- **D — Uttar Pradesh:** Famous for Nautanki, Raslila, and traditional pehlwani wrestling.
- **Exam Extra:** Gatka was included in the Khelo India Youth Games alongside Kalaripayattu, Thang-Ta, and Mallakhamb to promote indigenous Indian sports.

### Q510 · State Folk & Tribal Dances, Theatre & Martial Arts

Silambam, an ancient bamboo-staff martial art mentioned in Sangam literature, has its roots in which state?

A. Kerala
B. Andhra Pradesh
C. Karnataka
D. Tamil Nadu

**Answer:** D. Tamil Nadu
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — Tamil Nadu (Correct):** Silambam is a traditional weapon-based martial art of Tamil Nadu centered on agile footwork and combat with a seasoned bamboo staff.
- **A — Kerala:** Origin of Kalaripayattu and Parisakali.
- **B — Andhra Pradesh:** Home to Kathi Samu, a traditional sword-fighting martial art.
- **C — Karnataka:** Known for traditional Garadi akharas and Kolata stick dance.
- **Exam Extra:** References to Silambam staff fighting appear in ancient Tamil Sangam literature such as the Silappadikaram.

### Q511 · State Folk & Tribal Dances, Theatre & Martial Arts

Mudiyettu, a UNESCO-recognized ritual dance-drama depicting the battle between Goddess Kali and Darika, belongs to which state?

A. Tamil Nadu
B. Kerala
C. Odisha
D. Karnataka

**Answer:** B. Kerala
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — Kerala (Correct):** Mudiyettu is a traditional ritual theatre and dance-drama of Kerala depicting the mythological battle between Goddess Kali and the demon Darika, inscribed by UNESCO in 2010.
- **A — Tamil Nadu:** Traditional ritual theatre includes Terukoothu and Bhagavata Mela.
- **C — Odisha:** Traditional folk theatres include Danda Nata, Prahlad Nataka, and Mughal Tamasha.
- **D — Karnataka:** Traditional ritual spirit-worship performance is Bhoota Aradhane (Bhoota Kola).
- **Exam Extra:** Before a Mudiyettu performance, artists draw an elaborate five-colour floor portrait (Kalam) of Goddess Kali using natural powders (Kalamezhuthu).

### Q512 · State Folk & Tribal Dances, Theatre & Martial Arts

Jatra, a popular open-air folk theatre tradition rooted in the Bhakti movement, is predominantly associated with which state?

A. Bihar
B. Assam
C. West Bengal
D. Maharashtra

**Answer:** C. West Bengal
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — West Bengal (Correct):** Jatra is a popular travelling open-air folk theatre form of West Bengal (and parts of Odisha and Tripura) that grew out of the 16th-century Bhakti movement led by Chaitanya Mahaprabhu.
- **A — Bihar:** Traditional folk theatre forms include Bidesiya (popularized by Bhikhari Thakur) and Jat-Jatin.
- **B — Assam:** Traditional religious theatre is Ankiya Naat (Bhaona), created by Srimanta Sankardev.
- **D — Maharashtra:** Traditional folk theatres are Tamasha and Dashavatar.
- **Exam Extra:** Historically performed throughout the night in rural courtyards, Jatra blends dramatic dialogue with high-pitched orchestral music.

### Q513 · State Folk & Tribal Dances, Theatre & Martial Arts

Nati, which set a Guinness World Record for the largest folk dance performance during Kullu Dussehra, is native to which state?

A. Himachal Pradesh
B. Uttarakhand
C. Sikkim
D. Punjab

**Answer:** A. Himachal Pradesh
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — Himachal Pradesh (Correct):** Nati is the premier traditional folk dance of Himachal Pradesh; in October 2015, nearly 10,000 dancers performed Kulluvi Nati at Kullu Dussehra to set a Guinness World Record.
- **B — Uttarakhand:** Known for Chholiya, Jhora, Chanchari, and Langvir Nritya.
- **C — Sikkim:** Known for Chu Faat, Maruni, and Tamang Selo folk dances.
- **D — Punjab:** Famous for Bhangra, Giddha, Jhumar, and Sammi.
- **Exam Extra:** Kulluvi, Sirmauri, Kinnauri, and Mahasuvi are major regional variants of Nati across Himachal Pradesh.

### Q514 · Hindustani & Carnatic Music, Gharanas & Instruments

Mian Tansen, one of the Navaratnas of Akbar's court, is historically linked to the founding lineage of which Hindustani music gharana?

A. Kirana Gharana
B. Agra Gharana
C. Patiala Gharana
D. Gwalior Gharana

**Answer:** D. Gwalior Gharana
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — Gwalior Gharana (Correct):** Mian Tansen, one of Emperor Akbar's Navaratnas, trained under Swami Haridas and is foundational to the Gwalior Gharana, the oldest and mother gharana of Hindustani khayal singing.
- **A — Kirana Gharana:** Founded by Ustad Abdul Karim Khan and Ustad Abdul Wahid Khan, famous for swara-centric intonation.
- **B — Agra Gharana:** Traced to Haji Sujan Khan and popularized by Ustad Faiyaz Khan, blending dhrupad nom-tom with khayal.
- **C — Patiala Gharana:** Founded in the 19th century by Ustad Fateh Ali Khan and Ustad Ali Baksh Khan, famed for brisk taans.
- **Exam Extra:** The annual Tansen Samaroh (Tansen Music Festival) is held every December at Behat/Gwalior in Madhya Pradesh near his tomb.

### Q515 · Hindustani & Carnatic Music, Gharanas & Instruments

Which trio of saint-composers is revered as the 'Trinity of Carnatic Music'?

A. Tyagaraja, Muthuswami Dikshitar, and Syama Sastri
B. Purandara Dasa, Annamacharya, and Kanaka Dasa
C. Swathi Thirunal, Papanasam Sivan, and Subramania Bharati
D. Kabir, Surdas, and Mirabai

**Answer:** A. Tyagaraja, Muthuswami Dikshitar, and Syama Sastri
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — Tyagaraja, Muthuswami Dikshitar, and Syama Sastri (Correct):** Tyagaraja, Muthuswami Dikshitar, and Syama Sastri—all born in Tiruvarur in the 18th century—constitute the celebrated 'Trinity of Carnatic Music' who elevated the kriti form.
- **B — Purandara Dasa, Annamacharya, and Kanaka Dasa:** Earlier Haridasa and Vaggeyakara saint-composers of the 15th–16th centuries, not the Tiruvarur Trinity.
- **C — Swathi Thirunal, Papanasam Sivan, and Subramania Bharati:** Later 19th–20th century composers of South India outside the classical Trinity.
- **D — Kabir, Surdas, and Mirabai:** Prominent North Indian Bhakti poets and saints of the Nirguna and Saguna traditions.
- **Exam Extra:** Purandara Dasa (1484–1564), who preceded the Trinity, is revered as the 'Pitamaha' (Grandfather) of Carnatic music for structuring its foundational pedagogy.

### Q516 · Hindustani & Carnatic Music, Gharanas & Instruments

Bharat Ratna Pandit Bhimsen Joshi, celebrated for khayal vocalism and devotional abhangas, belonged to which Hindustani music gharana?

A. Jaipur-Atrauli Gharana
B. Kirana Gharana
C. Patiala Gharana
D. Gwalior Gharana

**Answer:** B. Kirana Gharana
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — Kirana Gharana (Correct):** Bharat Ratna Pandit Bhimsen Joshi (awarded 2008/conferred 2009) was a stalwart of the Kirana Gharana, renowned for khayal singing, Kannada and Marathi devotional abhangas, and the ragas Miyan ki Todi and Darbari.
- **A — Jaipur-Atrauli Gharana:** Founded by Ustad Alladiya Khan; famous exponents include Kesarbai Kerkar, Mogubai Kurdikar, and Kishori Amonkar.
- **C — Patiala Gharana:** Associated with Ustad Bade Ghulam Ali Khan, Begum Akhtar's ghazal influences, and Ajoy Chakrabarty.
- **D — Gwalior Gharana:** Associated with Vishnu Digambar Paluskar, Pandit Omkarnath Thakur, and Malini Rajurkar.
- **Exam Extra:** Other illustrious vocalists of the Kirana Gharana include Gangubai Hangal, Prabha Atre, and Roshan Ara Begum.

### Q517 · Hindustani & Carnatic Music, Gharanas & Instruments

Ustad Bade Ghulam Ali Khan, celebrated for his mastery of both khayal and thumri, was the foremost exponent of which gharana?

A. Agra Gharana
B. Rampur-Sahaswan Gharana
C. Patiala Gharana
D. Bhendi Bazaar Gharana

**Answer:** C. Patiala Gharana
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — Patiala Gharana (Correct):** Ustad Bade Ghulam Ali Khan was the towering 20th-century exponent of the Patiala Gharana, famed for his extraordinary vocal range, cascading taans, and iconic thumri 'Yaad Piya Ki Aaye.'
- **A — Agra Gharana:** Led in modern times by Ustad Faiyaz Khan ('Aftab-e-Mausiqi') and Vilayat Hussain Khan.
- **B — Rampur-Sahaswan Gharana:** Founded by Ustad Inayat Hussain Khan; notable modern exponents include Ustad Rashid Khan and Ghulam Mustafa Khan.
- **D — Bhendi Bazaar Gharana:** Founded in Mumbai by Chhajju Khan, Nazir Khan, and Khadim Hussain Khan, noted for breath control and merukhand patterns.
- **Exam Extra:** He was awarded the Padma Bhushan and Sangeet Natak Akademi Award in 1962.

### Q518 · Hindustani & Carnatic Music, Gharanas & Instruments

Who was the first musician in India to be awarded the Bharat Ratna?

A. M.S. Subbulakshmi
B. Pandit Ravi Shankar
C. Ustad Bismillah Khan
D. Lata Mangeshkar

**Answer:** A. M.S. Subbulakshmi
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — M.S. Subbulakshmi (Correct):** Carnatic vocalist M.S. Subbulakshmi became the first musician in India to be conferred the Bharat Ratna in 1998.
- **B — Pandit Ravi Shankar:** Legendary sitar virtuoso who received the Bharat Ratna in 1999, a year after M.S. Subbulakshmi.
- **C — Ustad Bismillah Khan:** Shehnai maestro who received the Bharat Ratna in 2001.
- **D — Lata Mangeshkar:** Iconic playback singer who received the Bharat Ratna jointly in 2001 alongside Bismillah Khan.
- **Exam Extra:** She was also the first Indian musician to receive the Ramon Magsaysay Award (1974) and performed at the United Nations General Assembly in 1966.

### Q519 · Hindustani & Carnatic Music, Gharanas & Instruments

Ustad Bismillah Khan, awarded the Bharat Ratna in 2001, brought classical concert stature to which wind instrument?

A. Bansuri
B. Nadaswaram
C. Harmonium
D. Shehnai

**Answer:** D. Shehnai
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — Shehnai (Correct):** Ustad Bismillah Khan elevated the shehnai—once confined to auspicious ceremonies and weddings—to a prestigious solo classical concert instrument, earning the Bharat Ratna in 2001.
- **A — Bansuri:** Bamboo flute popularized in classical music by Pannalal Ghosh and Hariprasad Chaurasia.
- **B — Nadaswaram:** Double-reed South Indian wind instrument played by maestros like T.N. Rajarathinam Pillai and Sheikh Chinna Maulana.
- **C — Harmonium:** Free-reed keyboard instrument played solo by stalwarts like Appa Jalgaonkar and Tulsidas Borkar.
- **Exam Extra:** Bismillah Khan famously performed shehnai from the ramparts of the Red Fort on the eve of India's Independence on August 15, 1947.

### Q520 · Hindustani & Carnatic Music, Gharanas & Instruments

Pandit Shivkumar Sharma pioneered the adaptation of which Kashmiri folk string instrument to Hindustani classical music?

A. Sarod
B. Santoor
C. Sitar
D. Sarangi

**Answer:** B. Santoor
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — Santoor (Correct):** Pandit Shivkumar Sharma modified and adapted the santoor—originally a hundred-stringed Kashmiri folk trapezoid hammer dulcimer—for full Hindustani classical ragas.
- **A — Sarod:** Fretless plucked lute played by Ustad Ali Akbar Khan and Ustad Amjad Ali Khan.
- **C — Sitar:** Fretted plucked lute associated with Pandit Ravi Shankar, Ustad Vilayat Khan, and Nikhil Banerjee.
- **D — Sarangi:** Bowed string instrument associated with Pandit Ram Narayan and Ustad Sabri Khan.
- **Exam Extra:** He teamed up with flutist Hariprasad Chaurasia as the famous duo 'Shiv-Hari,' composing classic film music as well.

### Q521 · Hindustani & Carnatic Music, Gharanas & Instruments

Ustad Amjad Ali Khan of the Senia Bangash gharana is a world-renowned virtuoso of which string instrument?

A. Sitar
B. Rudra Veena
C. Sarod
D. Surbahar

**Answer:** C. Sarod
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — Sarod (Correct):** Ustad Amjad Ali Khan of the Senia Bangash gharana is a Padma Vibhushan awardee and one of the world's foremost virtuosos of the fretless sarod.
- **A — Sitar:** Associated with maestros like Pandit Ravi Shankar, Ustad Vilayat Khan, and Anoushka Shankar.
- **B — Rudra Veena:** Ancient stick zither associated with Dhrupad maestros like Ustad Asad Ali Khan and Zia Mohiuddin Dagar.
- **D — Surbahar:** Bass sitar played by Ustad Imrat Khan and Annapurna Devi.
- **Exam Extra:** The sarod evolved from the Afghan rabab and features a polished steel fingerboard without frets, enabling continuous microtonal slides (meend).

### Q522 · Hindustani & Carnatic Music, Gharanas & Instruments

Pandit Hariprasad Chaurasia of the Maihar gharana is an internationally acclaimed maestro of which musical instrument?

A. Bansuri
B. Shehnai
C. Tabla
D. Violin

**Answer:** A. Bansuri
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — Bansuri (Correct):** Pandit Hariprasad Chaurasia, a Padma Vibhushan recipient who trained under Annapurna Devi of the Maihar gharana, is the leading modern exponent of the bansuri (bamboo flute).
- **B — Shehnai:** Double-reed aerophone associated with Ustad Bismillah Khan and Ali Ahmad Hussain Khan.
- **C — Tabla:** Percussion pair associated with Ustad Allah Rakha, Ustad Zakir Hussain, and Pandit Kishan Maharaj.
- **D — Violin:** Bowed instrument popularized in Indian classical music by L. Subramaniam, T.N. Krishnan, and N. Rajam.
- **Exam Extra:** Earlier in the 20th century, Pandit Pannalal Ghosh increased the length and bore of the bamboo flute to give it the deep tonal range required for classical ragas.

### Q523 · Hindustani & Carnatic Music, Gharanas & Instruments

Ustad Zakir Hussain, a four-time Grammy winner and Padma Vibhushan awardee, achieved global acclaim on which percussion instrument?

A. Pakhawaj
B. Mridangam
C. Ghatam
D. Tabla

**Answer:** D. Tabla
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — Tabla (Correct):** Ustad Zakir Hussain (1951–2024), son of Ustad Allah Rakha of the Punjab gharana, won four Grammy Awards and received the Padma Vibhushan (2023) for his mastery of the tabla.
- **A — Pakhawaj:** Double-headed barrel drum used in Dhrupad, played by maestros like Raja Chhatrapati Singh and Bhawani Shankar.
- **B — Mridangam:** Primary South Indian double-headed percussion drum associated with Palghat Mani Iyer and Umayalpuram K. Sivaraman.
- **C — Ghatam:** Clay-pot percussion instrument associated with Vikku Vinayakram.
- **Exam Extra:** The tabla consists of two drums: the smaller right-hand wooden drum (dayan) tuned to a specific pitch, and the larger left-hand metal bass drum (bayan).

### Q524 · Hindustani & Carnatic Music, Gharanas & Instruments

Pandit Ram Narayan is credited with elevating which bowed string instrument to a solo Hindustani classical concert instrument?

A. Esraj
B. Sarangi
C. Dilruba
D. Violin

**Answer:** B. Sarangi
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — Sarangi (Correct):** Pandit Ram Narayan (Padma Vibhushan, 2005) transformed the sarangi from a vocal accompaniment instrument into an independent solo concert instrument in Hindustani music.
- **A — Esraj:** Bowed string instrument popular in Bengal and Rabindra Sangeet traditions.
- **C — Dilruba:** Fretted bowed string instrument widely used in Sikh Shabad Kirtan.
- **D — Violin:** Introduced to Indian classical music in the 18th–19th century, notably adopted by Baluswami Dikshitar in Carnatic music.
- **Exam Extra:** The sarangi has 3 main gut strings and up to 35 sympathetic metal strings (tarab) that create its rich, voice-like resonance.

### Q525 · Hindustani & Carnatic Music, Gharanas & Instruments

Grammy-winning Carnatic musician Vikku Vinayakram is famously associated with which percussion instrument?

A. Kanjira
B. Morsing
C. Ghatam
D. Thavil

**Answer:** C. Ghatam
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — Ghatam (Correct):** T.H. 'Vikku' Vinayakram is an iconic Carnatic percussionist who brought global recognition and Grammy honours to the ghatam, a narrow-mouthed baked clay pot.
- **A — Kanjira:** Small frame drum with lizard-skin head and jingles, played by maestros like G. Harishankar.
- **B — Morsing:** South Indian jaw harp (jew's harp) played in Carnatic percussion ensembles.
- **D — Thavil:** Barrel-shaped drum commonly accompanying the nadaswaram in South Indian temple music.
- **Exam Extra:** Traditional ghatams are specially crafted in Manamadurai (Tamil Nadu) using clay mixed with iron or brass filings for metallic resonance.

### Q526 · Hindustani & Carnatic Music, Gharanas & Instruments

Kishori Amonkar, one of the foremost vocalists of the 20th century, belonged to which Hindustani music gharana?

A. Jaipur-Atrauli Gharana
B. Gwalior Gharana
C. Kirana Gharana
D. Mewati Gharana

**Answer:** A. Jaipur-Atrauli Gharana
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — Jaipur-Atrauli Gharana (Correct):** Kishori Amonkar (Padma Vibhushan, 2002) was a legendary vocalist of the Jaipur-Atrauli Gharana, inheriting the tradition from her mother, Mogubai Kurdikar.
- **B — Gwalior Gharana:** Oldest khayal gharana founded by Nathan Pir Baksh and Hassu-Haddu Khan.
- **C — Kirana Gharana:** Associated with Abdul Karim Khan, Bhimsen Joshi, and Gangubai Hangal.
- **D — Mewati Gharana:** Founded by Ustad Ghagge Nazir Khan and popularized globally by Pandit Jasraj.
- **Exam Extra:** The Jaipur-Atrauli Gharana was founded by Ustad Alladiya Khan in the late 19th century and is known for complex ragas (jod ragas) and intricate rhythmic phrasing.

### Q527 · Hindustani & Carnatic Music, Gharanas & Instruments

Under the Natyashastra classification of Indian musical instruments, wind instruments like the bansuri and shehnai fall under which category?

A. Tat Vadya
B. Avanaddha Vadya
C. Ghana Vadya
D. Sushira Vadya

**Answer:** D. Sushira Vadya
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — Sushira Vadya (Correct):** In Bharata Muni's Natyashastra four-fold instrument classification, Sushira Vadya refers to aerophones (wind instruments) such as the bansuri, shehnai, and nadaswaram.
- **A — Tat Vadya:** Stringed instruments (plucked or bowed) such as sitar, sarod, veena, and sarangi.
- **B — Avanaddha Vadya:** Membrane-covered percussion instruments such as tabla, mridangam, and pakhawaj.
- **C — Ghana Vadya:** Solid percussion instruments (idiophones) that do not need tuning, such as ghatam, manjeera, and jal tarang.
- **Exam Extra:** The four classical categories—Tat (chordophones), Avanaddha (membranophones), Ghana (idiophones), and Sushira (aerophones)—inspired the modern Hornbostel–Sachs system.

### Q528 · Temple, Cave & Mughal Architecture & Paintings

The Ajanta Caves, famous for their Buddhist rock-cut monuments and murals, are located in which state?

A. Madhya Pradesh
B. Maharashtra
C. Karnataka
D. Gujarat

**Answer:** B. Maharashtra
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — Maharashtra (Correct):** The Ajanta Caves, consisting of 30 rock-cut Buddhist cave monuments dating from the 2nd century BCE to c. 480 CE, are located in the Chhatrapati Sambhajinagar (Aurangabad) district of Maharashtra.
- **A — Madhya Pradesh:** Famous for the prehistoric Bhimbetka rock shelters and the Bagh Buddhist caves.
- **C — Karnataka:** Famous for the Chalukyan rock-cut cave temples at Badami and Aihole.
- **D — Gujarat:** Known for the Junagadh (Uparkot) and Khambhalida Buddhist caves.
- **Exam Extra:** Carved into a horseshoe-shaped gorge of the Waghora River, Ajanta was inscribed as one of India's first UNESCO World Heritage Sites in 1983.

### Q529 · Temple, Cave & Mughal Architecture & Paintings

The Konark Sun Temple, built by King Narasimhadeva I in the 13th century, is located in which state?

A. Andhra Pradesh
B. West Bengal
C. Odisha
D. Tamil Nadu

**Answer:** C. Odisha
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — Odisha (Correct):** The Konark Sun Temple, located on the coastline of Puri district in Odisha, was built in the 13th century CE by King Narasimhadeva I of the Eastern Ganga dynasty.
- **A — Andhra Pradesh:** Home to the Arasavalli Sun Temple in Srikakulam district and Lepakshi Veerabhadra Temple.
- **B — West Bengal:** Known for the terracotta temples of Bishnupur and Dakshineswar Kali Temple.
- **D — Tamil Nadu:** Known for the Suryanar Kovil (one of the Navagraha temples) and the Chola temples of Thanjavur.
- **Exam Extra:** Designed as a colossal stone chariot of Surya pulled by seven horses on 24 intricately carved wheels, it is nicknamed the 'Black Pagoda' and appears on the reverse of the Indian ₹10 banknote.

### Q530 · Temple, Cave & Mughal Architecture & Paintings

The monolithic Kailasa Temple (Cave 16) at Ellora was commissioned by which Rashtrakuta ruler?

A. Krishna I
B. Dantidurga
C. Amoghavarsha I
D. Govinda III

**Answer:** A. Krishna I
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — Krishna I (Correct):** The monolithic Kailasa Temple (Cave 16) at Ellora was excavated top-down from a single basalt cliff during the reign of Rashtrakuta King Krishna I (c. 756–773 CE).
- **B — Dantidurga:** Founder of the Rashtrakuta Empire who defeated the Chalukyas of Badami around 753 CE.
- **C — Amoghavarsha I:** Longest-reigning Rashtrakuta monarch and patron of Kannada literature who authored 'Kavirajamarga.'
- **D — Govinda III:** Powerful Rashtrakuta ruler famous for his successful campaigns in North India during the Tripartite Struggle.
- **Exam Extra:** Ellora has 34 caves representing three religions harmoniously: Buddhist (Caves 1–12), Hindu (Caves 13–29), and Jain (Caves 30–34).

### Q531 · Temple, Cave & Mughal Architecture & Paintings

The Brihadisvara Temple at Thanjavur, a masterpiece of Chola Dravida architecture, was built by which ruler?

A. Rajendra Chola I
B. Vijayalaya Chola
C. Kulottunga I
D. Rajaraja Chola I

**Answer:** D. Rajaraja Chola I
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — Rajaraja Chola I (Correct):** The Brihadisvara Temple (Rajarajesvaram) at Thanjavur, completed around 1010 CE, was built by Rajaraja Chola I and is part of UNESCO's 'Great Living Chola Temples.'
- **A — Rajendra Chola I:** Son of Rajaraja I who founded Gangaikonda Cholapuram and built a second Brihadisvara Temple there.
- **B — Vijayalaya Chola:** Founder of the Imperial Chola line in the 9th century who captured Thanjavur.
- **C — Kulottunga I:** Later Chalukya-Chola monarch who ruled in the late 11th and early 12th centuries.
- **Exam Extra:** Its massive 66-metre granite vimana is topped by a single octagonal stone block (kumbam/shikhara) weighing roughly 80 tonnes.

### Q532 · Temple, Cave & Mughal Architecture & Paintings

The Khajuraho Group of Monuments in Madhya Pradesh was built by which dynasty?

A. Paramara dynasty
B. Chandela dynasty
C. Solanki dynasty
D. Pratihara dynasty

**Answer:** B. Chandela dynasty
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — Chandela dynasty (Correct):** The Khajuraho Group of Monuments in Chhatarpur district, Madhya Pradesh, was built predominantly between 950 and 1050 CE by rulers of the Chandela dynasty.
- **A — Paramara dynasty:** Ruled Malwa (capital Dhara); famous for Raja Bhoja and the Bhojeshwar Temple at Bhojpur.
- **C — Solanki dynasty:** Solanki (Chaulukya) dynasty: Ruled Gujarat and built the Modhera Sun Temple and Rani ki Vav at Patan.
- **D — Pratihara dynasty:** Gurjara-Pratihara rulers of Kannauj known for early Nagara temples at Osian and Bateshwar.
- **Exam Extra:** Built in the Nagara style without mortar, the Kandariya Mahadeva Temple is the largest and most ornate among the surviving temples at Khajuraho.

### Q533 · Temple, Cave & Mughal Architecture & Paintings

Which Indian temple architecture style blends features of both the northern Nagara and southern Dravida traditions?

A. Kalinga style
B. Gadag style
C. Vesara style
D. Pala style

**Answer:** C. Vesara style
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — Vesara style (Correct):** The Vesara style (literally 'mule,' signifying a hybrid) combines architectural features of the northern Nagara and southern Dravida styles, flourishing under the Chalukyas, Rashtrakutas, and Hoysalas in the Deccan.
- **A — Kalinga style:** Sub-school of Nagara architecture in Odisha featuring Rekha Deula, Pidha Deula, and Khakhara Deula structures.
- **B — Gadag style:** A specific regional sub-type of Western Chalukyan temple architecture characterized by ornate pillars.
- **D — Pala style:** Architecture of Bengal and Bihar (8th–12th centuries) famous for Buddhist mahaviharas like Vikramashila and Somapura.
- **Exam Extra:** Star-shaped (stellate) ground plans and soapstone carvings are hallmarks of later Vesara (Hoysala) temples at Belur, Halebidu, and Somnathapura.

### Q534 · Temple, Cave & Mughal Architecture & Paintings

The Dilwara Jain Temples at Mount Abu, famed for their intricate white marble carvings, are situated in which state?

A. Rajasthan
B. Gujarat
C. Madhya Pradesh
D. Maharashtra

**Answer:** A. Rajasthan
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — Rajasthan (Correct):** The Dilwara Jain Temples (such as Vimal Vasahi and Luna Vasahi) are located at Mount Abu in Sirohi district, Rajasthan, renowned for translucent Makrana marble filigree work.
- **B — Gujarat:** Home to major Jain pilgrimage complexes at Palitana (Shatrunjaya Hills) and Girnar.
- **C — Madhya Pradesh:** Home to Jain temples at Khajuraho, Sonagiri, and Bawangaja.
- **D — Maharashtra:** Home to Jain caves at Ellora and the Mangi-Tungi shrine.
- **Exam Extra:** Built between the 11th and 13th centuries during the Chaulukya/Vaghela period, they showcase the Maru-Gurjara style of architecture.

### Q535 · Temple, Cave & Mughal Architecture & Paintings

The Modhera Sun Temple, constructed in the 11th century under Chaulukya king Bhima I, is located in which state?

A. Odisha
B. Rajasthan
C. Madhya Pradesh
D. Gujarat

**Answer:** D. Gujarat
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — Gujarat (Correct):** The Modhera Sun Temple, built in 1026–27 CE during the reign of Bhima I of the Chaulukya (Solanki) dynasty, stands on the banks of the Pushpavati River in Mehsana district, Gujarat.
- **A — Odisha:** Home to the 13th-century Konark Sun Temple built by Narasimhadeva I.
- **B — Rajasthan:** Home to the Surya Mandir at Osian and Ranakpur Sun Temple.
- **C — Madhya Pradesh:** Home to the Unao Balaji Sun Temple near Datia.
- **Exam Extra:** The temple's stepped tank, Surya Kund (Rama Kund), features 108 miniature shrines carved into its stone steps.

### Q536 · Temple, Cave & Mughal Architecture & Paintings

Pietra dura (Parchin Kari) stone-inlay decoration was first extensively used in India in which Mughal monument?

A. Humayun's Tomb
B. Tomb of Itimad-ud-Daulah
C. Bibi Ka Maqbara
D. Red Fort of Agra

**Answer:** B. Tomb of Itimad-ud-Daulah
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — Tomb of Itimad-ud-Daulah (Correct):** Pietra dura (Parchin Kari)—inlaying polished semi-precious stones like lapis lazuli, jasper, and carnelian into white marble—was first used extensively in India in the Tomb of Itimad-ud-Daulah in Agra.
- **A — Humayun's Tomb:** Built in Delhi in 1565–72 using red sandstone and white marble, pioneering the charbagh garden-tomb layout.
- **C — Bibi Ka Maqbara:** Built in Aurangabad (1660s) by Azam Shah in memory of his mother Dilras Banu Begum as a late Mughal imitation of the Taj Mahal.
- **D — Red Fort of Agra:** Built primarily of red sandstone by Akbar (1565–73), with later marble additions by Shah Jahan.
- **Exam Extra:** Built between 1622 and 1628 by Empress Nur Jahan for her father Mirza Ghiyas Beg, it is widely regarded as a precursor to the Taj Mahal.

### Q537 · Temple, Cave & Mughal Architecture & Paintings

Which monumental gateway at Fatehpur Sikri was built by Emperor Akbar to commemorate his victory over Gujarat?

A. Alai Darwaza
B. Rumi Darwaza
C. Buland Darwaza
D. Lahori Gate

**Answer:** C. Buland Darwaza
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — Buland Darwaza (Correct):** Buland Darwaza ('Door of Victory'), the 54-metre-high sandstone gateway at the southern entrance of the Jama Masjid in Fatehpur Sikri, was built by Akbar to commemorate his conquest of Gujarat.
- **A — Alai Darwaza:** Southern gateway of the Quwwat-ul-Islam Mosque at the Qutb Complex, Delhi, built by Alauddin Khalji in 1311.
- **B — Rumi Darwaza:** Iconic 18th-century gateway in Lucknow built by Nawab Asaf-ud-Daula of Awadh.
- **D — Lahori Gate:** Main western entrance to the Red Fort (Lal Qila) in Delhi, built by Shah Jahan.
- **Exam Extra:** It carries a famous Persian inscription attributed to Jesus: 'The world is a bridge, pass over it, but build no houses upon it.'

### Q538 · Temple, Cave & Mughal Architecture & Paintings

Madhubani (Mithila) painting, traditionally created using natural dyes and fine geometric patterns, originates from which state?

A. Bihar
B. Jharkhand
C. Uttar Pradesh
D. West Bengal

**Answer:** A. Bihar
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — Bihar (Correct):** Madhubani (Mithila) painting originated in the Mithila region of Bihar, traditionally painted by women using fingers, twigs, and natural pigments made from rice paste, turmeric, and indigo.
- **B — Jharkhand:** Famous for Sohrai and Kohvar tribal wall paintings of Hazaribagh.
- **C — Uttar Pradesh:** Known for Sanjhi paper-stencil art of Mathura and Chikankari embroidery.
- **D — West Bengal:** Known for Kalighat Pat painting and Alpana floor art.
- **Exam Extra:** Notable Padma awardee Madhubani artists include Sita Devi, Ganga Devi, Baua Devi, and Dulari Devi.

### Q539 · Temple, Cave & Mughal Architecture & Paintings

Warli painting, a tribal art using white rice paste and basic geometric shapes on mud walls, belongs to which state?

A. Odisha
B. Rajasthan
C. Chhattisgarh
D. Maharashtra

**Answer:** D. Maharashtra
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — Maharashtra (Correct):** Warli painting is a centuries-old tribal wall art of the Warli community in the Palghar–Thane region of Maharashtra, using white rice paste on red-ochre walls with basic geometric shapes (circle, triangle, square).
- **A — Odisha:** Known for Saura wall paintings (Idital) and Pattachitra scroll paintings.
- **B — Rajasthan:** Known for Phad scroll paintings, Pichwai of Nathdwara, and Mandana floor art.
- **C — Chhattisgarh:** Known for Gond and Pithora-influenced tribal murals and Bell Metal (Dhokra) art.
- **Exam Extra:** Jivya Soma Mashe was a Padma Shri awardee who popularized Warli painting on canvas internationally.

### Q540 · Temple, Cave & Mughal Architecture & Paintings

Pattachitra, a traditional cloth-based scroll painting style depicting narratives of Lord Jagannath, is primarily native to which state?

A. Andhra Pradesh
B. Odisha
C. Karnataka
D. Assam

**Answer:** B. Odisha
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — Odisha (Correct):** Pattachitra ('patta' meaning cloth, 'chitra' meaning picture) is a traditional scroll painting of Odisha famed for vivid mythological themes centred on Lord Jagannath and the Vaishnava tradition.
- **A — Andhra Pradesh:** Famous for Kalamkari (Srikalahasti and Machilipatnam styles) hand-painted cotton textiles.
- **C — Karnataka:** Famous for Mysore traditional painting using gesso work and gold leaf.
- **D — Assam:** Known for illuminated Vaishnava manuscripts (Sanchipat paintings) such as the Hastividyarnava.
- **Exam Extra:** Raghurajpur village near Puri is a famous heritage crafts village known for Pattachitra painting and Gotipua dance.

### Q541 · Festivals, Classical Languages & UNESCO Heritage Sites

Bihu, celebrated in three seasonal forms—Bohag, Kati, and Magh—is a major festival of which state?

A. Manipur
B. Tripura
C. Assam
D. Meghalaya

**Answer:** C. Assam
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — Assam (Correct):** Bihu is the defining cultural and agricultural festival of Assam, celebrated across three seasonal phases: Bohag (Rongali) Bihu in April, Kati (Kongali) Bihu in October, and Magh (Bhogali) Bihu in January.
- **A — Manipur:** Celebrates Yaoshang (spring festival), Cheiraoba (New Year), and Ningol Chakouba.
- **B — Tripura:** Celebrates Garia Puja, Kharchi Puja, and Ker Puja.
- **D — Meghalaya:** Celebrates Wangala, Shad Suk Mynsiem, and Behdienkhlam.
- **Exam Extra:** Bohag Bihu marks the Assamese New Year and features lively Bihu dances accompanied by the dhol, pepa (buffalo-horn pipe), and gogona.

### Q542 · Festivals, Classical Languages & UNESCO Heritage Sites

The Hornbill Festival, famously known as the 'Festival of Festivals' and held every December, is celebrated in which state?

A. Nagaland
B. Mizoram
C. Arunachal Pradesh
D. Manipur

**Answer:** A. Nagaland
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — Nagaland (Correct):** The Hornbill Festival, launched in 2000 and held every December 1–10 at Kisama Heritage Village near Kohima, showcases the heritage of all Naga tribes and is called the 'Festival of Festivals.'
- **B — Mizoram:** Major festivals include Chapchar Kut, Mim Kut, and Pawl Kut.
- **C — Arunachal Pradesh:** Celebrates Solung (Adi), Losar (Monpa), Nyokum (Nyishi), and Dree (Apatani).
- **D — Manipur:** Celebrates Sangai Festival, Lai Haraoba, and Shirui Lily Festival.
- **Exam Extra:** It is named after the Great Indian Hornbill, a bird deeply revered in Naga folklore and ceremonial headgear.

### Q543 · Festivals, Classical Languages & UNESCO Heritage Sites

Bathukamma, a vibrant floral festival celebrated predominantly by women during Navratri, is the state festival of which Indian state?

A. Andhra Pradesh
B. Karnataka
C. Kerala
D. Telangana

**Answer:** D. Telangana
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — Telangana (Correct):** Bathukamma ('Mother Goddess come alive') is a nine-day floral festival celebrated by women in Telangana during Durga Navratri, culminating on Saddula Bathukamma.
- **A — Andhra Pradesh:** Major regional festivals include Sankranti, Ugadi, and the Flamingo Festival at Pulicat Lake.
- **B — Karnataka:** Celebrates Mysuru Dasara, Kambala, Ugadi, and Karaga.
- **C — Kerala:** Celebrates Onam, Vishu, and Thrissur Pooram.
- **Exam Extra:** Women arrange seasonal wild flowers like gunuka (celosia) and tangedu (senna) in concentric layers into a conical stack and float them in water bodies.

### Q544 · Festivals, Classical Languages & UNESCO Heritage Sites

Nuakhai, an agrarian festival welcoming the new rice harvest of the season, is predominantly celebrated in western parts of which state?

A. Jharkhand
B. Odisha
C. Chhattisgarh
D. Bihar

**Answer:** B. Odisha
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — Odisha (Correct):** Nuakhai ('nua' meaning new, 'khai' meaning food) is an ancient harvest festival celebrated the day after Ganesh Chaturthi in western Odisha to offer the first rice grains (nabanna) to Goddess Samaleswari.
- **A — Jharkhand:** Celebrates Sarhul (spring festival of nature), Karma, and Sohrai.
- **C — Chhattisgarh:** Celebrates Hareli, Bastar Dussehra, and Madai festivals.
- **D — Bihar:** Celebrates Chhath Puja, Sama Chakeva, and Sonepur Mela.
- **Exam Extra:** A traditional custom during Nuakhai is 'Nuakhai Juhar,' where younger family members seek blessings from elders.

### Q545 · Festivals, Classical Languages & UNESCO Heritage Sites

Losar, marking the Tibetan Lunar New Year with mask dances and monastic rituals, is widely celebrated in which region of India?

A. Lakshadweep
B. Goa
C. Ladakh
D. Puducherry

**Answer:** C. Ladakh
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — Ladakh (Correct):** Losar is the traditional Tibetan Lunar New Year festival widely celebrated in Ladakh (as well as Sikkim and parts of Arunachal Pradesh and Himachal Pradesh) with monastic Cham mask dances and family feasts.
- **A — Lakshadweep:** Predominantly celebrates Eid-ul-Fitr, Eid-ul-Adha, and Milad-un-Nabi.
- **B — Goa:** Celebrates Shigmo (spring festival), Sao Joao, and Goa Carnival.
- **D — Puducherry:** Celebrates Pongal, Masi Magam, and Bastille Day cultural events.
- **Exam Extra:** In Ladakh, Hemis Tsechu is another major Buddhist monastic festival held in summer to commemorate the birth of Guru Padmasambhava.

### Q546 · Festivals, Classical Languages & UNESCO Heritage Sites

Wangala, also called the 'Festival of Hundred Drums,' is a post-harvest festival of the Garo tribe in which state?

A. Meghalaya
B. Assam
C. Tripura
D. Nagaland

**Answer:** A. Meghalaya
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — Meghalaya (Correct):** Wangala, known as the 'Festival of Hundred Drums,' is the major post-harvest thanksgiving festival of the Garo tribe in Meghalaya, honouring Saljong (the Sun God of fertility).
- **B — Assam:** Celebrates Ali-Ai-Ligang (Mishing tribe), Jonbeel Mela, and Ambubachi Mela.
- **C — Tripura:** Celebrates Hojagiri and Kharchi Puja (worship of 14 deities).
- **D — Nagaland:** Celebrates Hornbill, Moatsu (Ao tribe), and Sekrenyi (Angami tribe).
- **Exam Extra:** During Wangala, dancers perform the Katta Doka and Rugala rituals dressed in feathered headgear (do'me) to the beat of 100 dama drums.

### Q547 · Festivals, Classical Languages & UNESCO Heritage Sites

Lai Haraoba, meaning 'merry-making of the gods,' is a traditional ritual festival of the Meitei community in which state?

A. Mizoram
B. Assam
C. Sikkim
D. Manipur

**Answer:** D. Manipur
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — Manipur (Correct):** Lai Haraoba ('merry-making of the gods') is an ancient ritual festival celebrated by the Meitei community of Manipur to appease the Umang Lai (ancestral forest deities).
- **A — Mizoram:** Celebrates Chapchar Kut after jhum (slash-and-burn) clearing.
- **B — Assam:** Celebrates Bihu and Baishagu (Bodo New Year).
- **C — Sikkim:** Celebrates Saga Dawa, Losoong (Namsoong), and Pang Lhabsol.
- **Exam Extra:** Classical Manipuri dance and traditional martial rituals draw heavily from the sacred dance sequences preserved in Lai Haraoba.

### Q548 · Festivals, Classical Languages & UNESCO Heritage Sites

Which was the first language to be accorded Classical Language status by the Government of India in 2004?

A. Sanskrit
B. Tamil
C. Kannada
D. Telugu

**Answer:** B. Tamil
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — Tamil (Correct):** Tamil became the very first Indian language to be granted Classical Language status by the Government of India in October 2004.
- **A — Sanskrit:** Designated as a Classical Language in 2005, one year after Tamil.
- **C — Kannada:** Designated as a Classical Language in 2008 alongside Telugu.
- **D — Telugu:** Designated as a Classical Language in 2008 alongside Kannada.
- **Exam Extra:** It was followed by Sanskrit (2005), Kannada and Telugu (2008), Malayalam (2013), and Odia (2014), before five more were approved in October 2024.

### Q549 · Festivals, Classical Languages & UNESCO Heritage Sites

In October 2024, the Union Cabinet approved Classical Language status for five additional languages, raising India's total number of Classical Languages to how many?

A. 8
B. 9
C. 11
D. 14

**Answer:** C. 11
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 11 (Correct):** On October 3, 2024, the Union Cabinet approved Classical Language status for Marathi, Pali, Prakrit, Assamese, and Bengali, adding to the existing 6 languages to reach a total of 11.
- **A — 8:** Incorrect; adding 5 languages to the existing 6 yields 11, not 8.
- **B — 9:** Incorrect; nine would account for only three new additions rather than five.
- **D — 14:** Incorrect; 14 was the initial number of languages in the Eighth Schedule of the Constitution in 1950 (now 22), not Classical Languages.
- **Exam Extra:** Prior to October 2024, India had 6 Classical Languages: Tamil, Sanskrit, Kannada, Telugu, Malayalam, and Odia.

### Q550 · Festivals, Classical Languages & UNESCO Heritage Sites

Which site in Assam—representing the mound-burial system of the Ahom Dynasty—was inscribed in July 2024 as India's 43rd UNESCO World Heritage Site?

A. Moidams of Charaideo
B. Majuli River Island
C. Kamakhya Temple Complex
D. Sivasagar Rang Ghar

**Answer:** A. Moidams of Charaideo
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — Moidams of Charaideo (Correct):** In July 2024, 'Moidams – the Mound-Burial System of the Ahom Dynasty' at Charaideo in Assam was inscribed as India's 43rd UNESCO World Heritage Site.
- **B — Majuli River Island:** World's largest inhabited river island on the Brahmaputra in Assam, currently on UNESCO's Tentative List rather than inscribed as the 43rd site.
- **C — Kamakhya Temple Complex:** Famous Shakti Peetha on Nilachal Hill in Guwahati, Assam; not a UNESCO World Heritage Site.
- **D — Sivasagar Rang Ghar:** 18th-century Ahom royal amphitheatre in Assam; not individually inscribed as the 43rd UNESCO site.
- **Exam Extra:** Moidams are vaulted earthen royal burial mounds built over 600 years of Ahom rule (13th–19th century) and became Northeast India's first cultural UNESCO World Heritage Site.

---

## Chapter 9: Awards, Honours, Books & Authors

### Q551 · Bharat Ratna, Padma & Gallantry Awards

Who was the first woman to receive the Bharat Ratna?

A. Sarojini Naidu
B. Indira Gandhi
C. Mother Teresa
D. Aruna Asaf Ali

**Answer:** B. Indira Gandhi
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — Indira Gandhi (Correct):** Indira Gandhi became the first woman to receive the Bharat Ratna in 1971, following the 1971 war and creation of Bangladesh.
- **A — Sarojini Naidu:** Freedom fighter and poet known as the 'Nightingale of India'; she became the first woman Governor of an Indian state (United Provinces) but never received the Bharat Ratna.
- **C — Mother Teresa:** Received the Bharat Ratna in 1980, becoming the second woman to receive India's highest civilian honour.
- **D — Aruna Asaf Ali:** Freedom fighter known as the 'Grand Old Lady of the Independence Movement'; she received the Bharat Ratna posthumously in 1997.
- **Exam Extra:** Only five women have received the Bharat Ratna so far: Indira Gandhi (1971), Mother Teresa (1980), Aruna Asaf Ali (1997, posthumous), M. S. Subbulakshmi (1998), and Lata Mangeshkar (2001).

### Q552 · Bharat Ratna, Padma & Gallantry Awards

Who was the first non-Indian (foreign national) to be awarded the Bharat Ratna?

A. Nelson Mandela
B. Dalai Lama
C. Khan Abdul Ghaffar Khan
D. Aung San Suu Kyi

**Answer:** C. Khan Abdul Ghaffar Khan
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — Khan Abdul Ghaffar Khan (Correct):** Khan Abdul Ghaffar Khan, popularly known as 'Frontier Gandhi' and founder of the Khudai Khidmatgar movement, became the first non-Indian to receive the Bharat Ratna in 1987.
- **A — Nelson Mandela:** Anti-apartheid leader and former President of South Africa who became the second non-Indian to receive the Bharat Ratna in 1990.
- **B — Dalai Lama:** Tibetan spiritual leader who received the Nobel Peace Prize in 1989; he has not been conferred the Bharat Ratna.
- **D — Aung San Suu Kyi:** Myanmar political leader who won the Nobel Peace Prize in 1991 and the Jawaharlal Nehru Award for International Understanding (1993), not the Bharat Ratna.
- **Exam Extra:** Nelson Mandela became the second non-Indian recipient of the Bharat Ratna in 1990 (Mother Teresa received it in 1980 as a naturalised Indian citizen).

### Q553 · Bharat Ratna, Padma & Gallantry Awards

Who was the first sportsperson to receive the Bharat Ratna?

A. Sachin Tendulkar
B. Major Dhyan Chand
C. Milkha Singh
D. Viswanathan Anand

**Answer:** A. Sachin Tendulkar
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — Sachin Tendulkar (Correct):** Sachin Tendulkar became the first sportsperson (and youngest recipient, at age 40) to be awarded the Bharat Ratna in 2014 alongside scientist C. N. R. Rao.
- **B — Major Dhyan Chand:** Legendary field hockey player after whom India's highest sporting honour (Khel Ratna) was renamed in 2021; he has not received the Bharat Ratna.
- **C — Milkha Singh:** Track-and-field sprinter known as the 'Flying Sikh' who received the Padma Shri in 1959.
- **D — Viswanathan Anand:** Five-time World Chess Champion and the first recipient of the Rajiv Gandhi Khel Ratna Award (1991–92); he received the Padma Vibhushan in 2007.
- **Exam Extra:** Sports was formally added as an eligible field ('any field of human endeavour') for the Bharat Ratna only in December 2011.

### Q554 · Bharat Ratna, Padma & Gallantry Awards

Who was the first musician to be conferred the Bharat Ratna?

A. Lata Mangeshkar
B. Pandit Ravi Shankar
C. Ustad Bismillah Khan
D. M. S. Subbulakshmi

**Answer:** D. M. S. Subbulakshmi
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — M. S. Subbulakshmi (Correct):** Carnatic vocalist M. S. Subbulakshmi became the first musician to receive the Bharat Ratna in 1998.
- **A — Lata Mangeshkar:** Renowned playback singer who received the Bharat Ratna in 2001 alongside Ustad Bismillah Khan.
- **B — Pandit Ravi Shankar:** Sitar virtuoso who received the Bharat Ratna in 1999, one year after M. S. Subbulakshmi.
- **C — Ustad Bismillah Khan:** Legendary shehnai maestro who received the Bharat Ratna in 2001.
- **Exam Extra:** M. S. Subbulakshmi was also the first Indian musician to receive the Ramon Magsaysay Award (1974) and to perform at the United Nations General Assembly (1966).

### Q555 · Bharat Ratna, Padma & Gallantry Awards

Who was the first person to receive the Bharat Ratna posthumously?

A. Dr. Rajendra Prasad
B. Lal Bahadur Shastri
C. B. R. Ambedkar
D. Sardar Vallabhbhai Patel

**Answer:** B. Lal Bahadur Shastri
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — Lal Bahadur Shastri (Correct):** Former Prime Minister Lal Bahadur Shastri became the first person to be awarded the Bharat Ratna posthumously in 1966, after his death in Tashkent.
- **A — Dr. Rajendra Prasad:** India's first President received the Bharat Ratna in 1962 during his lifetime, shortly before leaving office.
- **C — B. R. Ambedkar:** Principal architect of the Indian Constitution who was awarded the Bharat Ratna posthumously in 1990.
- **D — Sardar Vallabhbhai Patel:** First Deputy Prime Minister and Home Minister of India who was awarded the Bharat Ratna posthumously in 1991.
- **Exam Extra:** The original 1954 statutes of the Bharat Ratna did not provide for posthumous awards; the rules were amended in January 1955 to allow posthumous conferral.

### Q556 · Bharat Ratna, Padma & Gallantry Awards

Which agricultural scientist, known as the 'Father of India's Green Revolution', was conferred the Bharat Ratna in 2024?

A. Verghese Kurien
B. Homi J. Bhabha
C. M. S. Swaminathan
D. Vikram Sarabhai

**Answer:** C. M. S. Swaminathan
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — M. S. Swaminathan (Correct):** M. S. Swaminathan, the agricultural scientist who spearheaded India's Green Revolution with high-yielding wheat and rice varieties, was posthumously conferred the Bharat Ratna in 2024.
- **A — Verghese Kurien:** Known as the 'Father of the White Revolution' (Operation Flood) in India; he received the Padma Vibhushan (1999) and Ramon Magsaysay Award (1963), not the Bharat Ratna.
- **B — Homi J. Bhabha:** Nuclear physicist regarded as the 'Father of the Indian Nuclear Programme' and founder of TIFR.
- **D — Vikram Sarabhai:** Physicist and astronomer regarded as the 'Father of the Indian Space Programme' and founder of ISRO's predecessor INCOSPAR.
- **Exam Extra:** M. S. Swaminathan also received the first World Food Prize in 1987, in addition to the Padma Shri, Padma Bhushan, and Padma Vibhushan.

### Q557 · Bharat Ratna, Padma & Gallantry Awards

Which former Chief Minister of Bihar, popularly known as 'Jan Nayak', was posthumously awarded the Bharat Ratna in 2024?

A. Karpoori Thakur
B. Sri Krishna Sinha
C. Bhola Paswan Shastri
D. Bindeshwari Dubey

**Answer:** A. Karpoori Thakur
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — Karpoori Thakur (Correct):** Karpoori Thakur, two-time Chief Minister of Bihar popularly known as 'Jan Nayak', was posthumously awarded the Bharat Ratna in 2024 on his birth centenary.
- **B — Sri Krishna Sinha:** First Chief Minister of Bihar (1946–1961), popularly known as 'Bihar Kesari'.
- **C — Bhola Paswan Shastri:** Served three terms as Chief Minister of Bihar and was the state's first Dalit Chief Minister.
- **D — Bindeshwari Dubey:** Former Chief Minister of Bihar (1985–1988) and Union Minister who did not receive the Bharat Ratna.
- **Exam Extra:** In 2024, five personalities received the Bharat Ratna: Karpoori Thakur, L. K. Advani, P. V. Narasimha Rao, Chaudhary Charan Singh, and M. S. Swaminathan.

### Q558 · Bharat Ratna, Padma & Gallantry Awards

Who among the following was NOT one of the first three recipients of the Bharat Ratna in 1954?

A. C. Rajagopalachari
B. Sarvepalli Radhakrishnan
C. C. V. Raman
D. Jawaharlal Nehru

**Answer:** D. Jawaharlal Nehru
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — Jawaharlal Nehru (Correct):** The first three recipients of the Bharat Ratna in 1954 were C. Rajagopalachari, Sarvepalli Radhakrishnan, and C. V. Raman; Jawaharlal Nehru received the award in 1955.
- **A — C. Rajagopalachari:** Last Governor-General of India and one of the three inaugural Bharat Ratna recipients in 1954.
- **B — Sarvepalli Radhakrishnan:** Noted philosopher and India's first Vice President (later second President), who received the inaugural Bharat Ratna in 1954.
- **C — C. V. Raman:** Nobel laureate physicist who was one of the three inaugural Bharat Ratna recipients in 1954.
- **Exam Extra:** The Bharat Ratna was instituted on 2 January 1954 by President Dr. Rajendra Prasad and carries no monetary grant, only a Sanad (certificate) and a Peepal-leaf-shaped medallion.

### Q559 · Bharat Ratna, Padma & Gallantry Awards

Which is the second-highest civilian award of India?

A. Bharat Ratna
B. Padma Vibhushan
C. Padma Bhushan
D. Padma Shri

**Answer:** B. Padma Vibhushan
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — Padma Vibhushan (Correct):** The Padma Vibhushan is the second-highest civilian award of the Republic of India, instituted in 1954 for exceptional and distinguished service.
- **A — Bharat Ratna:** The highest civilian award of India, ranking above the Padma Vibhushan.
- **C — Padma Bhushan:** The third-highest civilian award of India, given for distinguished service of high order.
- **D — Padma Shri:** The fourth-highest civilian award of India, given for distinguished service in any field.
- **Exam Extra:** The order of precedence of Indian civilian honours from highest to lowest is: Bharat Ratna, Padma Vibhushan, Padma Bhushan, and Padma Shri.

### Q560 · Bharat Ratna, Padma & Gallantry Awards

Which is the highest wartime gallantry award in India?

A. Param Vir Chakra
B. Maha Vir Chakra
C. Vir Chakra
D. Ashoka Chakra

**Answer:** A. Param Vir Chakra
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — Param Vir Chakra (Correct):** The Param Vir Chakra (PVC) is India's highest military decoration awarded for displaying distinguished acts of valour during wartime.
- **B — Maha Vir Chakra:** The second-highest wartime gallantry award in India, ranking immediately below the Param Vir Chakra.
- **C — Vir Chakra:** The third-highest wartime gallantry award in India.
- **D — Ashoka Chakra:** India's highest peacetime military decoration for valour, courageous action, or self-sacrifice away from the battlefield.
- **Exam Extra:** The Param Vir Chakra medal was designed by Savitri Khanolkar and features four replicas of Indra's Vajra flanking the State Emblem of India.

### Q561 · Bharat Ratna, Padma & Gallantry Awards

Which is the highest peacetime gallantry award in India?

A. Kirti Chakra
B. Shaurya Chakra
C. Ashoka Chakra
D. Param Vir Chakra

**Answer:** C. Ashoka Chakra
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — Ashoka Chakra (Correct):** The Ashoka Chakra is India's highest peacetime gallantry decoration, awarded for valour, courageous action, or self-sacrifice away from the battlefield.
- **A — Kirti Chakra:** The second-highest peacetime gallantry award in India, equivalent in precedence to the Maha Vir Chakra.
- **B — Shaurya Chakra:** The third-highest peacetime gallantry award in India, equivalent in precedence to the Vir Chakra.
