- **D — Param Vir Chakra:** India's highest wartime gallantry award, conferred for valour in the presence of the enemy.
- **Exam Extra:** Neerja Bhanot became the youngest recipient (and first woman) to be awarded the Ashoka Chakra posthumously in 1987.

### Q562 · Bharat Ratna, Padma & Gallantry Awards

Who was the first recipient of the Param Vir Chakra?

A. Captain Vikram Batra
B. Havildar Abdul Hamid
C. Second Lieutenant Arun Khetarpal
D. Major Somnath Sharma

**Answer:** D. Major Somnath Sharma
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — Major Somnath Sharma (Correct):** Major Somnath Sharma of the 4th Battalion, Kumaon Regiment, was the first recipient of the Param Vir Chakra, awarded posthumously for his bravery in the Battle of Badgam (November 1947).
- **A — Captain Vikram Batra:** Awarded the Param Vir Chakra posthumously for his heroism in the 1999 Kargil War (Operation Vijay).
- **B — Havildar Abdul Hamid:** Awarded the Param Vir Chakra posthumously for destroying Pakistani Patton tanks in the 1965 Indo-Pakistani War.
- **C — Second Lieutenant Arun Khetarpal:** Awarded the Param Vir Chakra posthumously for his tank battle heroism in the 1971 Indo-Pakistani War.
- **Exam Extra:** Out of 21 Param Vir Chakra awardees so far, 20 are from the Indian Army and 1 (Flying Officer Nirmal Jit Singh Sekhon, 1971) is from the Indian Air Force.

### Q563 · Cinema & Music Awards

The Dadasaheb Phalke Award is associated with which field?

A. Classical music
B. Literature
C. Indian cinema
D. Journalism

**Answer:** C. Indian cinema
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — Indian cinema (Correct):** The Dadasaheb Phalke Award is India's highest award in the field of cinema, presented annually at the National Film Awards for lifetime contribution to Indian cinema.
- **A — Classical music:** Recognised by honours such as the Sangeet Natak Akademi Award and the Tansen Samman.
- **B — Literature:** Recognised by major Indian literary honours such as the Jnanpith Award and the Sahitya Akademi Award.
- **D — Journalism:** Recognised by awards such as the Ramnath Goenka Excellence in Journalism Awards and the Pulitzer Prize internationally.
- **Exam Extra:** Instituted in 1969 in honour of Dhundiraj Govind Phalke (who directed India's first full-length feature film 'Raja Harishchandra' in 1913), the award carries a Swarna Kamal medallion, a shawl, and a cash prize.

### Q564 · Cinema & Music Awards

Who was the first recipient of the Dadasaheb Phalke Award in 1969?

A. Devika Rani
B. Prithviraj Kapoor
C. Satyajit Ray
D. Lata Mangeshkar

**Answer:** A. Devika Rani
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — Devika Rani (Correct):** Actress Devika Rani, often called the 'First Lady of Indian Cinema', was the first recipient of the Dadasaheb Phalke Award when it was instituted in 1969.
- **B — Prithviraj Kapoor:** Veteran stage and film actor who received the Dadasaheb Phalke Award posthumously in 1971 (for the year 1971).
- **C — Satyajit Ray:** Acclaimed filmmaker who received the Dadasaheb Phalke Award in 1984 (for the year 1984).
- **D — Lata Mangeshkar:** Legendary playback singer who received the Dadasaheb Phalke Award in 1989.
- **Exam Extra:** Mithun Chakraborty was conferred the Dadasaheb Phalke Award for the year 2022 (presented in October 2024), following Waheeda Rehman (2021).

### Q565 · Cinema & Music Awards

Who was the first Indian to win an Academy Award (Oscar)?

A. Satyajit Ray
B. Bhanu Athaiya
C. A. R. Rahman
D. Resul Pookutty

**Answer:** B. Bhanu Athaiya
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — Bhanu Athaiya (Correct):** Costume designer Bhanu Athaiya became the first Indian to win an Academy Award (Oscar) in 1983 for Best Costume Design in Richard Attenborough's film 'Gandhi'.
- **A — Satyajit Ray:** Received an Honorary Academy Award in 1992 for lifetime achievement, nine years after Bhanu Athaiya's win.
- **C — A. R. Rahman:** Won two Academy Awards in 2009 (Best Original Score and Best Original Song for 'Jai Ho' from 'Slumdog Millionaire').
- **D — Resul Pookutty:** Won the Academy Award for Best Sound Mixing in 2009 for 'Slumdog Millionaire'.
- **Exam Extra:** Satyajit Ray became the first Indian filmmaker to receive an Honorary Academy Award for Lifetime Achievement in 1992.

### Q566 · Cinema & Music Awards

Which Indian song won the Academy Award (Oscar) for Best Original Song in 2023?

A. Jai Ho
B. Chaiyya Chaiyya
C. Kesariya
D. Naatu Naatu

**Answer:** D. Naatu Naatu
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — Naatu Naatu (Correct):** 'Naatu Naatu' from S. S. Rajamouli's Telugu film 'RRR' (composed by M. M. Keeravani with lyrics by Chandrabose) won Best Original Song at the 95th Academy Awards in 2023.
- **A — Jai Ho:** Composed by A. R. Rahman with lyrics by Gulzar for 'Slumdog Millionaire', it won Best Original Song at the 81st Academy Awards in 2009.
- **B — Chaiyya Chaiyya:** Iconic song composed by A. R. Rahman for the 1998 film 'Dil Se..', never nominated for an Academy Award.
- **C — Kesariya:** Popular Hindi film song from 'Brahmastra' (2022) composed by Pritam, not nominated for an Academy Award.
- **Exam Extra:** At the same 95th Oscars (2023), Kartiki Gonsalves and Guneet Monga's 'The Elephant Whisperers' also won Best Documentary Short Film.

### Q567 · Cinema & Music Awards

Who was the first Indian musician to win a Grammy Award?

A. Pandit Ravi Shankar
B. Zakir Hussain
C. A. R. Rahman
D. Ricky Kej

**Answer:** A. Pandit Ravi Shankar
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — Pandit Ravi Shankar (Correct):** Sitar maestro Pandit Ravi Shankar became the first Indian musician to win a Grammy Award in 1968 for Best Chamber Music Performance for the album 'West Meets East' with Yehudi Menuhin.
- **B — Zakir Hussain:** Renowned tabla virtuoso who won his first Grammy in 1992 for 'Planet Drum' and won three Grammys in a single night in 2024.
- **C — A. R. Rahman:** Music composer who won two Grammy Awards in 2010 for 'Slumdog Millionaire' and 'Jai Ho'.
- **D — Ricky Kej:** Indian music composer who won his first Grammy Award in 2015 for 'Winds of Samsara' and has won three Grammys in total.
- **Exam Extra:** Pandit Ravi Shankar won a total of five Grammy Awards, including a Lifetime Achievement Grammy Award posthumously in 2013.

### Q568 · Sports Awards

The Arjuna Award recognises achievement in which field?

A. Literature
B. Sports
C. Defence services
D. Performing arts

**Answer:** B. Sports
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — Sports (Correct):** The Arjuna Award, instituted in 1961 by the Government of India, recognises outstanding achievement in national sports.
- **A — Literature:** Honoured by awards such as the Jnanpith Award, Sahitya Akademi Award, and Saraswati Samman.
- **C — Defence services:** Honoured by gallantry decorations such as the Param Vir Chakra and Ashoka Chakra, as well as distinguished service medals.
- **D — Performing arts:** Honoured by awards such as the Sangeet Natak Akademi Award for music, dance, and theatre.
- **Exam Extra:** Eligibility for the Arjuna Award requires consistent outstanding performance over the previous four years along with leadership, sportsmanship, and discipline.

### Q569 · Sports Awards

Which is the highest sporting honour in India?

A. Arjuna Award
B. Dronacharya Award
C. Major Dhyan Chand Khel Ratna Award
D. Maulana Abul Kalam Azad Trophy

**Answer:** C. Major Dhyan Chand Khel Ratna Award
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — Major Dhyan Chand Khel Ratna Award (Correct):** The Major Dhyan Chand Khel Ratna Award (formerly known as the Rajiv Gandhi Khel Ratna Award) is the highest sporting honour of India, awarded for spectacular and most outstanding performance over four years.
- **A — Arjuna Award:** India's oldest national sports award (instituted 1961), ranking below the Major Dhyan Chand Khel Ratna Award.
- **B — Dronacharya Award:** Conferred specifically on sports coaches for producing medal winners at prestigious international events.
- **D — Maulana Abul Kalam Azad Trophy:** Rolling trophy awarded annually to the overall top-performing university in inter-university sports tournaments.
- **Exam Extra:** Instituted in 1991–92 as the Rajiv Gandhi Khel Ratna Award, it was renamed after hockey legend Major Dhyan Chand in August 2021.

### Q570 · Sports Awards

Who was the first recipient of the Khel Ratna Award (1991–92)?

A. Geet Sethi
B. Sachin Tendulkar
C. Karnam Malleswari
D. Viswanathan Anand

**Answer:** D. Viswanathan Anand
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — Viswanathan Anand (Correct):** Chess Grandmaster Viswanathan Anand was the first recipient of the Rajiv Gandhi Khel Ratna Award when it was instituted for the year 1991–92.
- **A — Geet Sethi:** World champion in English billiards who received the Khel Ratna Award in 1992–93, making him the second recipient.
- **B — Sachin Tendulkar:** Batting legend who became the first cricketer to receive the Khel Ratna Award in 1997–98.
- **C — Karnam Malleswari:** Olympic bronze-medallist weightlifter who received the Khel Ratna Award in 1995–96.
- **Exam Extra:** Karnam Malleswari became the first woman weightlifter to receive the Khel Ratna Award (1995–96), and Sachin Tendulkar became the first cricketer to receive it (1997–98).

### Q571 · Sports Awards

The Dronacharya Award is conferred to recognise excellence in which field?

A. Sports coaching
B. Lifetime athletic performance
C. Inter-university sports championships
D. Adventure sports

**Answer:** A. Sports coaching
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — Sports coaching (Correct):** The Dronacharya Award, instituted in 1985, is presented by the Ministry of Youth Affairs and Sports to recognise excellence in sports coaching.
- **B — Lifetime athletic performance:** Recognised by the Major Dhyan Chand Award for Lifetime Achievement in Sports and Games (instituted in 2002).
- **C — Inter-university sports championships:** Recognised by the Maulana Abul Kalam Azad (MAKA) Trophy, instituted in 1956–57.
- **D — Adventure sports:** Recognised by the Tenzing Norgay National Adventure Award for land, sea, and air adventure.
- **Exam Extra:** The first recipients of the Dronacharya Award in 1985 were Bhalchandra Bhaskar Bhagwat (wrestling), Om Prakash Bhardwaj (boxing), and O. M. Nambiar (athletics).

### Q572 · Literary & Journalism Awards

Who was the first recipient of the Jnanpith Award in 1965?

A. Sumitranandan Pant
B. Umashankar Joshi
C. G. Sankara Kurup
D. Ramdhari Singh Dinkar

**Answer:** C. G. Sankara Kurup
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — G. Sankara Kurup (Correct):** Malayalam poet G. Sankara Kurup was the first recipient of the Jnanpith Award in 1965 for his collection of poems 'Odakkuzhal' (The Bamboo Flute).
- **A — Sumitranandan Pant:** Renowned Chhayavad poet who became the first Hindi writer to win the Jnanpith Award in 1968 for 'Chidambara'.
- **B — Umashankar Joshi:** Gujarati poet who jointly won the Jnanpith Award in 1967 with Kannada writer K. V. Puttappa (Kuvempu).
- **D — Ramdhari Singh Dinkar:** Noted Hindi poet who won the Jnanpith Award in 1972 for his epic poem 'Urvashi'.
- **Exam Extra:** Instituted in 1961 by the Bharatiya Jnanpith trust, the Jnanpith Award is India's oldest and highest literary honour, given for works in the 22 Eighth Schedule languages plus English.

### Q573 · Literary & Journalism Awards

Who was the first woman to win the Jnanpith Award?

A. Amrita Pritam
B. Ashapurna Devi
C. Mahadevi Varma
D. Mahasweta Devi

**Answer:** B. Ashapurna Devi
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — Ashapurna Devi (Correct):** Bengali novelist Ashapurna Devi became the first woman to win the Jnanpith Award in 1976 for her novel 'Pratham Pratisruti'.
- **A — Amrita Pritam:** Punjabi poet and novelist who became the second woman to win the Jnanpith Award in 1981 for 'Kagaz Te Canvas'.
- **C — Mahadevi Varma:** Hindi poet who became the third woman to win the Jnanpith Award in 1982 for 'Yama'.
- **D — Mahasweta Devi:** Noted Bengali writer and activist who received the Jnanpith Award in 1996.
- **Exam Extra:** Amitav Ghosh became the first English-language writer to receive the Jnanpith Award in 2018 (54th Jnanpith Award).

### Q574 · Literary & Journalism Awards

Which Indian author won the 2022 International Booker Prize for 'Tomb of Sand'?

A. Arundhati Roy
B. Kiran Desai
C. Aravind Adiga
D. Geetanjali Shree

**Answer:** D. Geetanjali Shree
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — Geetanjali Shree (Correct):** Geetanjali Shree won the 2022 International Booker Prize for 'Tomb of Sand', translated by Daisy Rockwell from her Hindi novel 'Ret Samadhi'—the first work in any Indian language to win the prize.
- **A — Arundhati Roy:** Won the Booker Prize in 1997 for her debut English novel 'The God of Small Things'.
- **B — Kiran Desai:** Won the Booker Prize in 2006 for her English novel 'The Inheritance of Loss'.
- **C — Aravind Adiga:** Won the Booker Prize in 2008 for his debut English novel 'The White Tiger'.
- **Exam Extra:** The International Booker Prize is awarded annually to a work of fiction translated into English and published in the UK or Ireland, with the prize money split equally between author and translator.

### Q575 · Nobel & International Awards

Who became the first Asian to win the Nobel Prize in Literature in 1913 for 'Gitanjali'?

A. Rabindranath Tagore
B. Rudyard Kipling
C. V. S. Naipaul
D. Salman Rushdie

**Answer:** A. Rabindranath Tagore
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — Rabindranath Tagore (Correct):** Rabindranath Tagore won the Nobel Prize in Literature in 1913 for his collection of poems 'Gitanjali' (Song Offerings), becoming the first non-European and first Asian Nobel laureate.
- **B — Rudyard Kipling:** British author born in Bombay who won the Nobel Prize in Literature in 1907 for works including 'The Jungle Book' and 'Kim'.
- **C — V. S. Naipaul:** Trinidad-born British writer of Indian descent who won the Nobel Prize in Literature in 2001.
- **D — Salman Rushdie:** Indian-born British-American novelist who won the 1981 Booker Prize for 'Midnight's Children', but has not won the Nobel Prize.
- **Exam Extra:** W. B. Yeats wrote the introduction to the English edition of 'Gitanjali' (1912), which brought Tagore's poetry to international prominence.

### Q576 · Nobel & International Awards

In which year did C. V. Raman win the Nobel Prize in Physics for the Raman Effect?

A. 1913
B. 1928
C. 1930
D. 1932

**Answer:** C. 1930
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 1930 (Correct):** Sir Chandrasekhara Venkata Raman won the Nobel Prize in Physics in 1930 for his discovery of the scattering of light, known as the Raman Effect.
- **A — 1913:** The year Rabindranath Tagore won the Nobel Prize in Literature for 'Gitanjali'.
- **B — 1928:** The year C. V. Raman discovered the Raman Effect in Kolkata, two years before receiving the Nobel Prize.
- **D — 1932:** The year James Chadwick discovered the neutron (for which Chadwick won the 1935 Physics Nobel), not the year of C. V. Raman's Nobel Prize.
- **Exam Extra:** C. V. Raman discovered the Raman Effect on 28 February 1928; this date is celebrated every year in India as National Science Day.

### Q577 · Nobel & International Awards

Who was the first Indian to receive the Ramon Magsaysay Award in 1958?

A. Mother Teresa
B. Acharya Vinoba Bhave
C. Verghese Kurien
D. Satyajit Ray

**Answer:** B. Acharya Vinoba Bhave
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — Acharya Vinoba Bhave (Correct):** Acharya Vinoba Bhave, leader of the Bhoodan Movement, became the first Indian to win the Ramon Magsaysay Award in 1958 for Community Leadership.
- **A — Mother Teresa:** Received the Ramon Magsaysay Award in 1962 for International Understanding, four years after Vinoba Bhave.
- **C — Verghese Kurien:** Received the Ramon Magsaysay Award in 1963 for Community Leadership for India's cooperative dairy movement.
- **D — Satyajit Ray:** Received the Ramon Magsaysay Award in 1967 for Journalism, Literature, and Creative Communication Arts.
- **Exam Extra:** Established in 1957 in memory of former Philippine President Ramon Magsaysay, the Ramon Magsaysay Award is often referred to as 'Asia's Nobel Prize'.

### Q578 · Nobel & International Awards

The Shanti Swarup Bhatnagar Prize is awarded for outstanding contributions in which field?

A. Literature and poetry
B. Classical dance and music
C. Economics and social work
D. Science and technology

**Answer:** D. Science and technology
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — Science and technology (Correct):** The Shanti Swarup Bhatnagar Prize is awarded annually by the Council of Scientific and Industrial Research (CSIR) for notable and outstanding research in science and technology in India.
- **A — Literature and poetry:** Recognised by Indian awards such as the Jnanpith Award, Sahitya Akademi Award, and Vyas Samman.
- **B — Classical dance and music:** Recognised by awards such as the Sangeet Natak Akademi Award and Kalidas Samman.
- **C — Economics and social work:** Recognised internationally by awards such as the Nobel Memorial Prize in Economic Sciences and the Ramon Magsaysay Award.
- **Exam Extra:** Instituted in 1958 in honour of CSIR founder-director Dr. Shanti Swarup Bhatnagar, the prize is given to Indian scientists below the age of 45 across seven scientific disciplines.

### Q579 · Ancient & Medieval Classic Books

Who wrote the Sanskrit play "Abhijnanashakuntalam"?

A. Kalidasa
B. Bhasa
C. Bhavabhuti
D. Shudraka

**Answer:** A. Kalidasa
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — Kalidasa (Correct):** 'Abhijnanashakuntalam' (The Recognition of Shakuntala) is one of the greatest Sanskrit dramas composed by the classical poet and playwright Kalidasa.
- **B — Bhasa:** Ancient Sanskrit dramatist famous for plays such as 'Swapnavasavadattam' and 'Pratijnayaugandharayana'.
- **C — Bhavabhuti:** 8th-century Sanskrit dramatist known for 'Uttaramacharita' and 'Malatimadhava'.
- **D — Shudraka:** Classical Sanskrit playwright credited with the social drama 'Mrichchhakatika' (The Little Clay Cart).
- **Exam Extra:** Kalidasa, often called the 'Shakespeare of India', was one of the Navaratnas (Nine Gems) at the court of Gupta Emperor Chandragupta II (Vikramaditya).

### Q580 · Ancient & Medieval Classic Books

Who authored the ancient Sanskrit treatise 'Arthashastra' on statecraft and economics?

A. Patanjali
B. Megasthenes
C. Kautilya
D. Vishakhadatta

**Answer:** C. Kautilya
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — Kautilya (Correct):** The 'Arthashastra', an ancient Indian Sanskrit treatise on statecraft, economic policy, and military strategy, was authored by Kautilya (also known as Chanakya or Vishnugupta).
- **A — Patanjali:** Ancient scholar who authored the 'Mahabhashya' (commentary on Sanskrit grammar) and the 'Yoga Sutras'.
- **B — Megasthenes:** Greek ambassador of Seleucus I Nicator to Chandragupta Maurya's court who wrote 'Indica'.
- **D — Vishakhadatta:** Sanskrit playwright who wrote 'Mudrarakshasa', a historical play depicting Chandragupta Maurya's rise to power.
- **Exam Extra:** The manuscript of the 'Arthashastra' was rediscovered and first published in 1909 by R. Shamasastry in Mysore.

### Q581 · Ancient & Medieval Classic Books

Who wrote 'Indica', an account of Mauryan India?

A. Fa-Hien
B. Megasthenes
C. Hiuen Tsang
D. Marco Polo

**Answer:** B. Megasthenes
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — Megasthenes (Correct):** 'Indica' was written by Megasthenes, the Greek ambassador sent by Seleucus I Nicator to the court of Chandragupta Maurya in Pataliputra.
- **A — Fa-Hien:** Chinese Buddhist pilgrim who visited India during the reign of Chandragupta II and recorded his travels in 'Fo-Kwo-Ki'.
- **C — Hiuen Tsang:** Chinese Buddhist monk who visited India during Harshavardhana's reign and authored 'Si-Yu-Ki'.
- **D — Marco Polo:** Venetian merchant-traveller who visited southern India in the late 13th century and described it in his travelogue.
- **Exam Extra:** Although the original Greek text of 'Indica' is lost, extensive fragments survive through quotations by later classical writers such as Strabo, Arrian, and Diodorus.

### Q582 · Ancient & Medieval Classic Books

Who is the author of 'Ashtadhyayi', the foundational treatise on Sanskrit grammar?

A. Patanjali
B. Katyayana
C. Aryabhata
D. Panini

**Answer:** D. Panini
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — Panini (Correct):** 'Ashtadhyayi' ('Eight Chapters'), the foundational treatise on Sanskrit grammar containing nearly 4,000 sutras, was composed by Panini.
- **A — Patanjali:** Authored the 'Mahabhashya' ('Great Commentary') on Panini's 'Ashtadhyayi' and Katyayana's 'Varttikas'.
- **B — Katyayana:** Ancient grammarian who wrote the 'Varttika' supplementary rules and commentaries on Panini's 'Ashtadhyayi'.
- **C — Aryabhata:** Classical Indian mathematician and astronomer who authored the 'Aryabhatiya' in 499 CE.
- **Exam Extra:** Panini's 'Ashtadhyayi' is regarded as the earliest known generative and formal linguistic grammar in the world.

### Q583 · Ancient & Medieval Classic Books

Who wrote 'Harshacharita' and 'Kadambari'?

A. Banabhatta
B. Harisena
C. Ravikirti
D. Dandin

**Answer:** A. Banabhatta
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — Banabhatta (Correct):** 'Harshacharita' (the biography of Emperor Harshavardhana) and the Sanskrit prose romance 'Kadambari' were written by Banabhatta, Harsha's court poet (Asthana Kavi).
- **B — Harisena:** Court poet of Gupta Emperor Samudragupta who composed the Prayaga Prashasti (Allahabad Pillar Inscription).
- **C — Ravikirti:** Court poet of Chalukya king Pulakeshin II who composed the Aihole inscription.
- **D — Dandin:** Classical Sanskrit prose writer and poet who authored 'Dashakumaracharita' and 'Kavyadarsha'.
- **Exam Extra:** Banabhatta died before finishing 'Kadambari', and the concluding portion was completed by his son Bhushanabhatta.

### Q584 · Ancient & Medieval Classic Books

Who composed 'Rajatarangini', the Sanskrit chronicle of the kings of Kashmir?

A. Bilhana
B. Somadeva
C. Kalhana
D. Jonaraja

**Answer:** C. Kalhana
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — Kalhana (Correct):** 'Rajatarangini' ('River of Kings'), a metrical historical chronicle of the kings of Kashmir written in Sanskrit, was composed by Kalhana in the 12th century CE.
- **A — Bilhana:** 11th-century Kashmiri poet who wrote 'Vikramankadevacharita', a eulogy of Chalukya king Vikramaditya VI.
- **B — Somadeva:** 11th-century Kashmiri writer who compiled the famous Sanskrit story collection 'Kathasaritsagara'.
- **D — Jonaraja:** 15th-century Kashmiri historian who wrote 'Dvitiya Rajatarangini' as a continuation of Kalhana's chronicle.
- **Exam Extra:** Kalhana completed 'Rajatarangini' around 1148–1150 CE during the reign of King Jayasimha of the Lohara dynasty.

### Q585 · Ancient & Medieval Classic Books

Who wrote the historical Sanskrit play 'Mudrarakshasa'?

A. Kalidasa
B. Vishakhadatta
C. Bhasa
D. Harsha

**Answer:** B. Vishakhadatta
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — Vishakhadatta (Correct):** 'Mudrarakshasa' ('The Signet of the Minister') is a historical Sanskrit play written by Vishakhadatta, depicting Chanakya's political strategy in placing Chandragupta Maurya on the throne.
- **A — Kalidasa:** Classical Gupta-era dramatist who wrote 'Abhijnanashakuntalam', 'Malavikagnimitram', and 'Vikramorvashiyam'.
- **C — Bhasa:** Early Sanskrit playwright credited with 'Swapnavasavadattam' and 'Urubhanga'.
- **D — Harsha:** 7th-century emperor of Kannauj who authored the three Sanskrit plays 'Ratnavali', 'Priyadarshika', and 'Nagananda'.
- **Exam Extra:** Vishakhadatta also wrote 'Devichandraguptam', a Sanskrit drama about Gupta ruler Chandragupta II's defeat of a Shaka ruler.

### Q586 · Ancient & Medieval Classic Books

Who authored the 12th-century Sanskrit lyrical poem 'Geet Govinda'?

A. Tulsidas
B. Kabir
C. Surdas
D. Jayadeva

**Answer:** D. Jayadeva
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — Jayadeva (Correct):** 'Geet Govinda', the 12th-century Sanskrit lyrical poem celebrating the divine love of Radha and Krishna, was composed by Jayadeva.
- **A — Tulsidas:** 16th-century Bhakti poet-saint who composed the Awadhi epic 'Ramcharitmanas' and 'Vinaya Patrika'.
- **B — Kabir:** 15th-century mystic poet-saint whose verses are compiled in the 'Bijak' and the 'Guru Granth Sahib'.
- **C — Surdas:** 16th-century blind Bhakti poet known for his Braj-language devotional compositions in 'Sur Sagar'.
- **Exam Extra:** Jayadeva was a court poet of King Lakshmana Sena of the Sena dynasty in Bengal.

### Q587 · Ancient & Medieval Classic Books

Who composed 'Buddha Charita', the Sanskrit epic biography of Gautama Buddha?

A. Ashvaghosha
B. Nagarjuna
C. Vasubandhu
D. Buddhaghosa

**Answer:** A. Ashvaghosha
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — Ashvaghosha (Correct):** 'Buddha Charita' (Acts of the Buddha), an epic Sanskrit biography of Gautama Buddha, was composed by the Buddhist poet-philosopher Ashvaghosha in the 1st–2nd century CE.
- **B — Nagarjuna:** Mahayana Buddhist philosopher who founded the Madhyamaka school and wrote 'Mulamadhyamakakarika'.
- **C — Vasubandhu:** Influential 4th–5th century Buddhist scholar who authored the 'Abhidharmakosha'.
- **D — Buddhaghosa:** 5th-century Theravada Buddhist commentator who wrote the 'Visuddhimagga' in Pali.
- **Exam Extra:** Ashvaghosha adorned the court of Kushan Emperor Kanishka and also authored 'Saundarananda' and 'Shariputraprakarana'.

### Q588 · Ancient & Medieval Classic Books

Who authored 'Ain-i-Akbari' and 'Akbarnama'?

A. Faizi
B. Abdul Qadir Badauni
C. Abul Fazl
D. Nizamuddin Ahmad

**Answer:** C. Abul Fazl
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — Abul Fazl (Correct):** 'Ain-i-Akbari' (Administration of Akbar), which forms the third volume of the 'Akbarnama', was written in Persian by Abul Fazl, one of Akbar's Navaratnas.
- **A — Faizi:** Elder brother of Abul Fazl and Akbar's poet laureate, known for translating Bhaskaracharya's 'Lilavati' into Persian.
- **B — Abdul Qadir Badauni:** Mughal historian and Akbar's critic who wrote 'Muntakhab-ut-Tawarikh'.
- **D — Nizamuddin Ahmad:** Mughal historian during Akbar's reign who authored 'Tabaqat-i-Akbari'.
- **Exam Extra:** The first two volumes of the 'Akbarnama' narrate the history of Akbar's ancestors and reign, while the third volume ('Ain-i-Akbari') details his administration, revenue, and culture.

### Q589 · Ancient & Medieval Classic Books

Who wrote 'Humayun-nama', the chronicle of Emperor Humayun's reign?

A. Nur Jahan
B. Gulbadan Begum
C. Jahanara Begum
D. Zeb-un-Nissa

**Answer:** B. Gulbadan Begum
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — Gulbadan Begum (Correct):** 'Humayun-nama', a vivid Persian account of the lives of Babur and Emperor Humayun, was written by Humayun's half-sister Gulbadan Begum at the request of Akbar.
- **A — Nur Jahan:** Influential Mughal empress and wife of Emperor Jahangir, known for her political acumen and patronage of arts.
- **C — Jahanara Begum:** Eldest daughter of Shah Jahan who authored the Sufi biographies 'Munis-al-Arwah' and 'Risala-i-Sahibiya'.
- **D — Zeb-un-Nissa:** Eldest daughter of Aurangzeb, renowned as a Sufi poetess writing in Persian under the pen name 'Makhfi'.
- **Exam Extra:** Gulbadan Begum was the daughter of Emperor Babur and is one of the very few female chroniclers of the Mughal imperial era.

### Q590 · Ancient & Medieval Classic Books

Who wrote 'Kitab-ul-Hind' (Tahqiq-i-Hind)?

A. Ibn Battuta
B. Amir Khusrau
C. Ziauddin Barani
D. Al-Biruni

**Answer:** D. Al-Biruni
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — Al-Biruni (Correct):** 'Kitab-ul-Hind' (also known as 'Tahqiq-i-Hind'), a comprehensive Arabic treatise on Indian religion, philosophy, science, and society, was written by Al-Biruni in the 11th century.
- **A — Ibn Battuta:** 14th-century Moroccan traveller who visited India during Muhammad bin Tughlaq's reign and wrote the 'Rihla'.
- **B — Amir Khusrau:** Indo-Persian Sufi poet and chronicler who wrote 'Tughlaqnama' and 'Khazain-ul-Futuh'.
- **C — Ziauddin Barani:** 14th-century Delhi Sultanate historian who wrote 'Tarikh-i-Firoz Shahi' and 'Fatwa-i-Jahandari'.
- **Exam Extra:** Al-Biruni accompanied Mahmud of Ghazni to India and learned Sanskrit to translate and study Indian texts directly.

### Q591 · Modern Freedom Era Books & Autobiographies

Who wrote "The Discovery of India"?

A. Mahatma Gandhi
B. Sardar Vallabhbhai Patel
C. Jawaharlal Nehru
D. Dr. Rajendra Prasad

**Answer:** C. Jawaharlal Nehru
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — Jawaharlal Nehru (Correct):** 'The Discovery of India' was written by Jawaharlal Nehru between 1942 and 1945 while he was imprisoned at Ahmednagar Fort during the Quit India Movement.
- **A — Mahatma Gandhi:** Authored 'Hind Swaraj' (1909) and his autobiography 'The Story of My Experiments with Truth'.
- **B — Sardar Vallabhbhai Patel:** First Deputy Prime Minister and Home Minister of India known for integrating the princely states; he did not write 'The Discovery of India'.
- **D — Dr. Rajendra Prasad:** First President of India who authored 'India Divided' (1946) and 'Atmakatha'.
- **Exam Extra:** Published in 1946, 'The Discovery of India' was later adapted into the 53-episode Doordarshan television series 'Bharat Ek Khoj' directed by Shyam Benegal.

### Q592 · Modern Freedom Era Books & Autobiographies

Who is the author of 'Hind Swaraj' (Indian Home Rule)?

A. Mahatma Gandhi
B. Gopal Krishna Gokhale
C. Vinoba Bhave
D. Annie Besant

**Answer:** A. Mahatma Gandhi
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — Mahatma Gandhi (Correct):** 'Hind Swaraj' (Indian Home Rule) was written by Mahatma Gandhi in 1909 in Gujarati aboard the ship SS Kildonan Castle on his return journey from London to South Africa.
- **B — Gopal Krishna Gokhale:** Moderate nationalist leader and Gandhi's political mentor who founded the Servants of India Society in 1905.
- **C — Vinoba Bhave:** Gandhian leader of the Bhoodan Movement who authored 'Gita Pravachane'.
- **D — Annie Besant:** Theosophist and Home Rule League leader who edited the newspapers 'New India' and 'Commonweal'.
- **Exam Extra:** When the British government banned the Gujarati edition of 'Hind Swaraj' in 1910, Gandhi himself translated it into English so its message could circulate widely.

### Q593 · Modern Freedom Era Books & Autobiographies

Who wrote 'Gita Rahasya' during his imprisonment in Mandalay?

A. Aurobindo Ghosh
B. Bal Gangadhar Tilak
C. Lala Lajpat Rai
D. Bipin Chandra Pal

**Answer:** B. Bal Gangadhar Tilak
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — Bal Gangadhar Tilak (Correct):** 'Gita Rahasya' (Shrimad Bhagavadgita Rahasya) was written in Marathi by Bal Gangadhar Tilak in 1910–1911 while serving a six-year prison sentence in Mandalay, Burma.
- **A — Aurobindo Ghosh:** Revolutionary-turned-philosopher who wrote 'The Life Divine', 'Essays on the Gita', and 'Savitri'.
- **C — Lala Lajpat Rai:** Extremist nationalist leader known as 'Punjab Kesari' who authored 'Unhappy India' and 'Young India'.
- **D — Bipin Chandra Pal:** Nationalist leader of the Lal-Bal-Pal trio who authored 'The Soul of India'.
- **Exam Extra:** In 'Gita Rahasya', Tilak interpreted the Bhagavad Gita as a philosophy of Karma Yoga (selfless action) rather than renunciation.

### Q594 · Modern Freedom Era Books & Autobiographies

Who is the author of 'Anandamath', which contains the song 'Vande Mataram'?

A. Sarat Chandra Chattopadhyay
B. Michael Madhusudan Dutt
C. Ishwar Chandra Vidyasagar
D. Bankim Chandra Chattopadhyay

**Answer:** D. Bankim Chandra Chattopadhyay
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — Bankim Chandra Chattopadhyay (Correct):** 'Anandamath', the 1882 Bengali novel set against the backdrop of the Sannyasi Rebellion and containing India's national song 'Vande Mataram', was written by Bankim Chandra Chattopadhyay.
- **A — Sarat Chandra Chattopadhyay:** Noted Bengali novelist who authored classics such as 'Devdas', 'Parineeta', and 'Pather Dabi'.
- **B — Michael Madhusudan Dutt:** 19th-century Bengali poet and dramatist famous for the epic 'Meghnad Badh Kavya'.
- **C — Ishwar Chandra Vidyasagar:** Bengali polymath and social reformer who authored the Bengali primer 'Borno Porichoy'.
- **Exam Extra:** The song 'Vande Mataram' was first sung publicly at the 1896 Calcutta session of the Indian National Congress by Rabindranath Tagore.

### Q595 · Modern Freedom Era Books & Autobiographies

Who wrote 'The Indian Struggle' (1920–1942)?

A. Subhas Chandra Bose
B. Bhagat Singh
C. Jawaharlal Nehru
D. Surendranath Banerjee

**Answer:** A. Subhas Chandra Bose
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — Subhas Chandra Bose (Correct):** 'The Indian Struggle, 1920–1942' is a two-part historical account of India's freedom movement written by Subhas Chandra Bose.
- **B — Bhagat Singh:** Revolutionary freedom fighter who wrote the famous essay 'Why I Am an Atheist' in Lahore Central Jail.
- **C — Jawaharlal Nehru:** First Prime Minister of India who authored 'Glimpses of World History' and 'An Autobiography'.
- **D — Surendranath Banerjee:** Moderate nationalist leader who authored the memoir 'A Nation in Making'.
- **Exam Extra:** The first part of 'The Indian Struggle' (covering 1920–1934) was published in London in 1935 and immediately banned by the British government in India.

### Q596 · Modern Freedom Era Books & Autobiographies

Who authored 'India Wins Freedom'?

A. C. Rajagopalachari
B. V. D. Savarkar
C. Maulana Abul Kalam Azad
D. Sarvepalli Radhakrishnan

**Answer:** C. Maulana Abul Kalam Azad
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — Maulana Abul Kalam Azad (Correct):** 'India Wins Freedom' is the autobiographical political account of the Indian independence movement and Partition authored by Maulana Abul Kalam Azad.
- **A — C. Rajagopalachari:** Last Governor-General of India who authored English retellings of the 'Mahabharata' and 'Ramayana'.
- **B — V. D. Savarkar:** Nationalist writer and revolutionary who authored 'The Indian War of Independence of 1857'.
- **D — Sarvepalli Radhakrishnan:** Philosopher-statesman who authored 'Indian Philosophy' and 'The Hindu View of Life'.
- **Exam Extra:** Maulana Abul Kalam Azad served as India's first Minister of Education (1947–1958), and his birthday (11 November) is celebrated as National Education Day.

### Q597 · Modern Freedom Era Books & Autobiographies

Who wrote 'Poverty and Un-British Rule in India'?

A. Romesh Chunder Dutt
B. Dadabhai Naoroji
C. Mahadev Govind Ranade
D. Dinshaw Wacha

**Answer:** B. Dadabhai Naoroji
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — Dadabhai Naoroji (Correct):** 'Poverty and Un-British Rule in India' (1901) was written by Dadabhai Naoroji, presenting his famous 'Drain of Wealth' theory explaining India's economic exploitation under British rule.
- **A — Romesh Chunder Dutt:** Economic historian and civil servant who authored 'The Economic History of India'.
- **C — Mahadev Govind Ranade:** Nationalist reformer and economist who authored 'Essays on Indian Economics'.
- **D — Dinshaw Wacha:** Co-founder of the Indian National Congress and prominent economic commentator from Bombay.
- **Exam Extra:** Known as the 'Grand Old Man of India', Dadabhai Naoroji was the first Indian elected to the British House of Commons (1892).

### Q598 · Modern Freedom Era Books & Autobiographies

Who is the author of 'Annihilation of Caste'?

A. Jyotiba Phule
B. E. V. Ramasamy Periyar
C. Narayana Guru
D. B. R. Ambedkar

**Answer:** D. B. R. Ambedkar
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — B. R. Ambedkar (Correct):** 'Annihilation of Caste' (1936) was written by Dr. B. R. Ambedkar as an undelivered presidential address prepared for the Jat-Pat Todak Mandal conference in Lahore.
- **A — Jyotiba Phule:** 19th-century social reformer from Maharashtra who founded the Satyashodhak Samaj and wrote 'Gulamgiri' (1873).
- **B — E. V. Ramasamy Periyar:** Social reformer who founded the Self-Respect Movement in Tamil Nadu in 1925.
- **C — Narayana Guru:** Spiritual leader and social reformer of Kerala who propagated the motto 'One Caste, One Religion, One God for Mankind'.
- **Exam Extra:** Dr. B. R. Ambedkar also founded the newspapers 'Mooknayak' (1920) and 'Bahishkrit Bharat' (1927), and authored 'The Buddha and His Dhamma'.

### Q599 · Modern Freedom Era Books & Autobiographies

'Playing It My Way' is the autobiography of which Indian cricketer?

A. Sachin Tendulkar
B. Sourav Ganguly
C. Yuvraj Singh
D. Sunil Gavaskar

**Answer:** A. Sachin Tendulkar
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — Sachin Tendulkar (Correct):** 'Playing It My Way' (published in 2014) is the autobiography of Indian batting legend Sachin Tendulkar, co-authored with Boria Majumdar.
- **B — Sourav Ganguly:** Former Indian cricket captain whose autobiography is titled 'A Century Is Not Enough'.
- **C — Yuvraj Singh:** Indian all-rounder whose memoir is titled 'The Test of My Life: From Cricket to Cancer and Back'.
- **D — Sunil Gavaskar:** Legendary Indian opener whose autobiography is titled 'Sunny Days'.
- **Exam Extra:** Sachin Tendulkar is the only cricketer to have scored 100 international centuries and the first male cricketer to score a double century in ODIs (200* vs South Africa, 2010).

### Q600 · Modern Freedom Era Books & Autobiographies

'The Race of My Life' is the autobiography of which Indian sportsperson?

A. P. T. Usha
B. Abhinav Bindra
C. Milkha Singh
D. Major Dhyan Chand

**Answer:** C. Milkha Singh
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — Milkha Singh (Correct):** 'The Race of My Life' (2013) is the autobiography of legendary Indian track-and-field sprinter Milkha Singh, co-authored with his daughter Sonia Sanwalka.
- **A — P. T. Usha:** Sprint queen of Indian athletics whose autobiography is titled 'Golden Girl'.
- **B — Abhinav Bindra:** India's first individual Olympic gold medallist whose autobiography is titled 'A Shot at History'.
- **D — Major Dhyan Chand:** Field hockey legend whose autobiography is titled 'Goal'.
- **Exam Extra:** Milkha Singh won gold medals in the 400 metres at the 1958 Commonwealth Games and the 1958 & 1962 Asian Games, and finished fourth in the 1960 Rome Olympics 400 m final.

---

## Chapter 10: Sports, Games & Important Days

### Q601 · Olympics & Paralympics (1896-2024 Paris)

In which year and city were the first Modern Olympic Games held?

A. 1894, Paris
B. 1896, Athens
C. 1900, Paris
D. 1904, St. Louis

**Answer:** B. 1896, Athens
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 1896, Athens (Correct):** The first Modern Olympic Games were held from April 6 to 15, 1896 at the Panathenaic Stadium in Athens, Greece, revived by Baron Pierre de Coubertin.
- **A — 1894, Paris:** Year (23 June 1894) when the International Olympic Committee (IOC) was founded in Paris, celebrated annually as Olympic Day.
- **C — 1900, Paris:** Hosted the 2nd Modern Olympics, where women participated for the first time and India debuted via Norman Pritchard.
- **D — 1904, St. Louis:** Hosted the 3rd Modern Summer Olympics, the first edition held outside Europe.
- **Exam Extra:** Ancient Olympic Games began in 776 BCE at Olympia, Greece in honour of Zeus and were abolished in 393 CE by Roman Emperor Theodosius I.

### Q602 · Olympics & Paralympics (1896-2024 Paris)

What do the five interlocking rings on the Olympic flag represent?

A. Five founding nations
B. Five original Olympic sports
C. Five inhabited continents
D. Five ideals of ancient Greece

**Answer:** C. Five inhabited continents
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — Five inhabited continents (Correct):** The five interlocking rings (Blue, Yellow, Black, Green, and Red) on a white background represent the union of the five inhabited continents of the world.
- **A — Five founding nations:** Fourteen nations competed at the inaugural 1896 Athens Olympics, not five.
- **B — Five original Olympic sports:** Nine sports were contested at the 1896 Athens Games, including athletics, gymnastics, swimming, and wrestling.
- **D — Five ideals of ancient Greece:** The Olympic motto consists of three core Latin ideals ('Citius, Altius, Fortius'), with 'Communiter' added in 2021.
- **Exam Extra:** Designed by Pierre de Coubertin in 1913, the Olympic flag was first hoisted at the 1920 Antwerp Olympics; the ring colours correspond to Europe (Blue), Asia (Yellow), Africa (Black), Oceania (Green), and the Americas (Red).

### Q603 · Olympics & Paralympics (1896-2024 Paris)

Who was the first Indian to win an individual Olympic medal after independence?

A. K. D. Jadhav
B. Milkha Singh
C. Leander Paes
D. Karnam Malleswari

**Answer:** A. K. D. Jadhav
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — K. D. Jadhav (Correct):** Wrestler K. D. Jadhav won a Bronze medal in the Men's Freestyle 57kg event at the 1952 Helsinki Olympics, becoming Independent India's first individual Olympic medalist.
- **B — Milkha Singh:** Finished 4th in the Men's 400m final at the 1960 Rome Olympics, missing a Bronze medal by 0.1 seconds.
- **C — Leander Paes:** Won Men's Singles Tennis Bronze at the 1996 Atlanta Olympics, ending India's 44-year drought for an individual Olympic medal.
- **D — Karnam Malleswari:** Won Weightlifting Bronze at the 2000 Sydney Olympics, becoming the first Indian woman to win an Olympic medal.
- **Exam Extra:** Before independence, Anglo-Indian athlete Norman Pritchard won two Silver medals (200m and 200m hurdles) for India at the 1900 Paris Olympics.

### Q604 · Olympics & Paralympics (1896-2024 Paris)

Who captained the Indian Men's Field Hockey team to its eighth and most recent Olympic Gold medal at Moscow 1980?

A. Jaipal Singh Munda
B. Major Dhyan Chand
C. Ajit Pal Singh
D. Vasudevan Baskaran

**Answer:** D. Vasudevan Baskaran
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — Vasudevan Baskaran (Correct):** Vasudevan Baskaran captained the Indian Men's Hockey team to its eighth and most recent Olympic Gold medal at Moscow 1980, defeating Spain 4-3 in the final.
- **A — Jaipal Singh Munda:** Captained the Indian Hockey team to its first-ever Olympic Gold medal at the 1928 Amsterdam Games.
- **B — Major Dhyan Chand:** Captained India to its third consecutive Olympic Hockey Gold medal at the 1936 Berlin Games, beating Germany 8-1.
- **C — Ajit Pal Singh:** Captained India to its only Men's Hockey World Cup title in 1975 at Kuala Lumpur.
- **Exam Extra:** Indian Field Hockey has won 13 Olympic medals overall (8 Gold, 1 Silver, 4 Bronze), including six consecutive Gold medals from 1928 to 1956.

### Q605 · Olympics & Paralympics (1896-2024 Paris)

At which Olympic Games did Abhinav Bindra win India's first-ever individual Olympic Gold medal?

A. 2004 Athens
B. 2008 Beijing
C. 2012 London
D. 2016 Rio

**Answer:** B. 2008 Beijing
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 2008 Beijing (Correct):** Abhinav Bindra won India's first-ever individual Olympic Gold medal in the Men's 10m Air Rifle Shooting event at the 2008 Beijing Olympics.
- **A — 2004 Athens:** Rajyavardhan Singh Rathore won India's first post-independence individual Silver medal in Men's Double Trap Shooting.
- **C — 2012 London:** India won 6 medals (2 Silver, 4 Bronze) without a Gold, including Vijay Kumar's Silver and Gagan Narang's Bronze in Shooting.
- **D — 2016 Rio:** India won 2 medals—Silver by P. V. Sindhu (Badminton) and Bronze by Sakshi Malik (Wrestling).
- **Exam Extra:** At 2008 Beijing, India also won two Bronze medals via Vijender Singh (Boxing 75kg) and Sushil Kumar (Wrestling 66kg).

### Q606 · Olympics & Paralympics (1896-2024 Paris)

Who was the first Indian woman to win two individual Olympic medals?

A. Karnam Malleswari
B. Saina Nehwal
C. M. C. Mary Kom
D. P. V. Sindhu

**Answer:** D. P. V. Sindhu
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — P. V. Sindhu (Correct):** P. V. Sindhu became the first Indian woman to win two individual Olympic medals by winning Women's Singles Badminton Silver at Rio 2016 and Bronze at Tokyo 2020.
- **A — Karnam Malleswari:** Won a single Olympic medal (Bronze in Women's 69kg Weightlifting) at the 2000 Sydney Olympics.
- **B — Saina Nehwal:** Won India's first Olympic Badminton medal (Bronze) at the 2012 London Olympics.
- **C — M. C. Mary Kom:** Won a single Olympic Bronze medal in Women's Flyweight (51kg) Boxing at the 2012 London Olympics.
- **Exam Extra:** Among Indian men, wrestler Sushil Kumar (2008 Bronze, 2012 Silver) and javelin thrower Neeraj Chopra (2020 Gold, 2024 Silver) have won two individual Olympic medals post-independence.

### Q607 · Olympics & Paralympics (1896-2024 Paris)

Which Indian athlete won Gold at the 2020 Tokyo Olympics with a throw of 87.58 metres, giving India its first Athletics Olympic Gold?

A. Neeraj Chopra
B. Bajrang Punia
C. Ravi Kumar Dahiya
D. Sumit Antil

**Answer:** A. Neeraj Chopra
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — Neeraj Chopra (Correct):** Neeraj Chopra threw 87.58 metres in Men's Javelin Throw at the 2020 Tokyo Olympics on 7 August 2021 to win India's first-ever Olympic Gold medal in Athletics.
- **B — Bajrang Punia:** Won a Bronze medal in Men's Freestyle 65kg Wrestling at the 2020 Tokyo Olympics.
- **C — Ravi Kumar Dahiya:** Won a Silver medal in Men's Freestyle 57kg Wrestling at the 2020 Tokyo Olympics.
- **D — Sumit Antil:** Won Gold in Men's Javelin Throw F64 at the 2020 Tokyo Paralympics with a world record throw of 68.55 m.
- **Exam Extra:** India's 2020 Tokyo campaign remains its best-ever Summer Olympics haul with 7 medals (1 Gold, 2 Silver, 4 Bronze), and August 7 is celebrated as National Javelin Day.

### Q608 · Olympics & Paralympics (1896-2024 Paris)

At the 2024 Paris Olympics, who became the first Indian post-independence to win two medals in a single Olympic edition?

A. Swapnil Kusale
B. Sarabjot Singh
C. Manu Bhaker
D. Aman Sehrawat

**Answer:** C. Manu Bhaker
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — Manu Bhaker (Correct):** Shooter Manu Bhaker won two Bronze medals at Paris 2024—in Women's 10m Air Pistol and 10m Air Pistol Mixed Team—becoming the first Indian post-independence to win two medals in a single Olympic Games.
- **A — Swapnil Kusale:** Won a single Bronze medal in Men's 50m Rifle 3 Positions Shooting at Paris 2024.
- **B — Sarabjot Singh:** Won one Bronze medal at Paris 2024 alongside Manu Bhaker in the 10m Air Pistol Mixed Team event.
- **D — Aman Sehrawat:** Won one Bronze medal in Men's 57kg Wrestling at Paris 2024, becoming India's youngest individual Olympic medalist at age 21.
- **Exam Extra:** India finished the 2024 Paris Olympics with 6 medals (1 Silver by Neeraj Chopra and 5 Bronze) and ranked 71st overall.

### Q609 · Olympics & Paralympics (1896-2024 Paris)

Who won India's first-ever Paralympic Gold medal at the 1972 Heidelberg Paralympics?

A. Devendra Jhajharia
B. Murlikant Petkar
C. Mariyappan Thangavelu
D. Joginder Singh Bedi

**Answer:** B. Murlikant Petkar
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — Murlikant Petkar (Correct):** Murlikant Petkar won India's first-ever Paralympic medal and Gold at the 1972 Heidelberg Paralympics in Men's 50m Freestyle Swimming with a World Record time of 37.33 seconds.
- **A — Devendra Jhajharia:** Won Paralympic Javelin Golds at 2004 Athens and 2016 Rio, plus Silver at 2020 Tokyo.
- **C — Mariyappan Thangavelu:** Won Men's High Jump T42 Gold at 2016 Rio, Silver at 2020 Tokyo, and Bronze at 2024 Paris.
- **D — Joginder Singh Bedi:** Won 1 Silver and 2 Bronze medals in Athletics at the 1984 Stoke Mandeville/New York Paralympics.
- **Exam Extra:** India made its Summer Paralympic debut at 1968 Tel Aviv, and Deepa Malik became the first Indian woman Paralympic medalist with a Shot Put Silver at 2016 Rio.

### Q610 · Olympics & Paralympics (1896-2024 Paris)

How many total medals did India win in its record-breaking campaign at the Paris 2024 Paralympic Games?

A. 19
B. 24
C. 29
D. 34

**Answer:** C. 29
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 29 (Correct):** India won an all-time record 29 medals (7 Gold, 9 Silver, 13 Bronze) at the Paris 2024 Paralympic Games, finishing 18th in the overall medal table.
- **A — 19:** India's previous record Paralympic medal tally set at the 2020 Tokyo Paralympics (5 Gold, 8 Silver, 6 Bronze).
- **B — 24:** Five fewer than India's Paris 2024 tally; India jumped directly from 19 medals in Tokyo to 29 in Paris.
- **D — 34:** Five more than India's actual Paris 2024 Paralympic haul of 29 medals.
- **Exam Extra:** At Paris 2024 Paralympics, Avani Lekhara became the first Indian woman to win two Paralympic Golds, and Harvinder Singh won India's first-ever Paralympic Gold in Archery.

### Q611 · Olympics & Paralympics (1896-2024 Paris)

Which city will host the 2028 Summer Olympic Games, where T20 Cricket is scheduled to return to the Olympics?

A. Los Angeles
B. Brisbane
C. Milan
D. London

**Answer:** A. Los Angeles
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — Los Angeles (Correct):** Los Angeles, USA will host the 34th Summer Olympic Games in 2028, marking the return of Cricket (in T20 format) to the Olympics for the first time since 1900.
- **B — Brisbane:** Selected to host the 35th Summer Olympic Games in 2032, Australia's third Summer Olympics after 1956 and 2000.
- **C — Milan:** Co-hosted (with Cortina d'Ampezzo, Italy) the 25th Winter Olympic Games in February 2026.
- **D — London:** Hosted the Summer Olympic Games three times in 1908, 1948, and 2012.
- **Exam Extra:** Along with T20 Cricket, Baseball/Softball, Squash, Flag Football, and Lacrosse (Sixes) have been added to the 2028 Los Angeles Olympic programme.

### Q612 · Asian Games & Commonwealth Games

Who coined the official motto of the Asian Games, 'Ever Onward'?

A. Guru Dutt Sondhi
B. Sir Dorabji Tata
C. Maharaja Yadavindra Singh
D. Pt. Jawaharlal Nehru

**Answer:** D. Pt. Jawaharlal Nehru
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — Pt. Jawaharlal Nehru (Correct):** India's first Prime Minister, Pt. Jawaharlal Nehru, coined the official motto of the Asian Games, 'Ever Onward', before the inaugural 1951 edition in New Delhi.
- **A — Guru Dutt Sondhi:** Known as the 'Father of the Asian Games' for proposing the Games and helping establish the Asian Games Federation.
- **B — Sir Dorabji Tata:** Served as the first President of the Indian Olympic Association (IOA) when it was founded in 1927.
- **C — Maharaja Yadavindra Singh:** Served as President of the IOA during the inaugural 1951 Asian Games in New Delhi.
- **Exam Extra:** The Asian Games are governed by the Olympic Council of Asia (OCA), headquartered in Kuwait City, and India is one of seven countries to compete in every edition.

### Q613 · Asian Games & Commonwealth Games

Who won India's first-ever Gold medal at the inaugural 1951 Asian Games in New Delhi?

A. Milkha Singh
B. Sachin Nag
C. K. D. Jadhav
D. Lavy Pinto

**Answer:** B. Sachin Nag
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — Sachin Nag (Correct):** Swimmer Sachin Nag won India's first-ever Asian Games Gold medal in the Men's 100m Freestyle event at the inaugural 1951 New Delhi Asian Games.
- **A — Milkha Singh:** Won Asian Games Gold medals in 200m and 400m later at the 1958 Tokyo and 1962 Jakarta editions.
- **C — K. D. Jadhav:** Won Independent India's first individual Olympic medal (Wrestling Bronze) at 1952 Helsinki.
- **D — Lavy Pinto:** Won 100m and 200m sprint Gold medals at the 1951 New Delhi Asian Games after Sachin Nag's swimming victory.
- **Exam Extra:** India finished 2nd overall at the 1951 New Delhi Asian Games with 51 medals (15 Gold, 16 Silver, 20 Bronze).

### Q614 · Asian Games & Commonwealth Games

Who was the first Indian woman to win a Gold medal at the Asian Games?

A. Kamaljit Sandhu
B. P. T. Usha
C. Mary D'Souza
D. Shiny Abraham

**Answer:** A. Kamaljit Sandhu
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — Kamaljit Sandhu (Correct):** Kamaljit Sandhu won Gold in the Women's 400m race at the 1970 Bangkok Asian Games, becoming the first Indian woman to win an Asian Games Gold medal.
- **B — P. T. Usha:** Won 4 Gold medals at the 1986 Seoul Asian Games after winning two Silver medals at the 1982 New Delhi edition.
- **C — Mary D'Souza:** Won India's first women's Asian Games medals (100m Bronze and 4x100m Silver) at 1951 New Delhi, but not Gold.
- **D — Shiny Abraham:** Won Women's 800m Gold at the 1986 Seoul Asian Games.
- **Exam Extra:** At the 2018 Jakarta-Palembang Asian Games, Vinesh Phogat became the first Indian woman wrestler to win an Asian Games Gold medal.

### Q615 · Asian Games & Commonwealth Games

How many total medals did India win in its historic campaign at the 19th Asian Games in Hangzhou (held in 2023)?

A. 70
B. 85
C. 101
D. 107

**Answer:** D. 107
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 107 (Correct):** India won a record 107 medals (28 Gold, 38 Silver, 41 Bronze) at the 19th Asian Games in Hangzhou (held in 2023), crossing the 100-medal mark for the first time and finishing 4th.
- **A — 70:** India's total medal tally at the 18th Asian Games in Jakarta-Palembang 2018 (16 Gold, 23 Silver, 31 Bronze).
- **B — 85:** India's medal total at the 20th Asian Games in Aichi-Nagoya 2026 (21 Gold, 27 Silver, 37 Bronze).
- **C — 101:** India's all-time best medal haul at the 2010 New Delhi Commonwealth Games, not the Asian Games.
- **Exam Extra:** The Hangzhou Asian Games motto was 'Heart to Heart, @Future', and both Indian Men's and Women's Cricket teams won Gold medals on their Asiad debut.

### Q616 · Asian Games & Commonwealth Games

What was the official mascot of the 1982 New Delhi Asian Games?

A. Shera
B. Savaj
C. Appu
D. Perry the Bull

**Answer:** C. Appu
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — Appu (Correct):** Appu, a cheerful baby elephant with a bindi on his forehead, served as the official mascot of the 9th Asian Games hosted by New Delhi in 1982.
- **A — Shera:** Official mascot (a Royal Bengal Tiger) of the 2010 New Delhi Commonwealth Games.
- **B — Savaj:** Official mascot (an Asiatic Lion) of the 36th National Games hosted by Gujarat in 2022.
- **D — Perry the Bull:** Official mascot of the 2022 Birmingham Commonwealth Games.
- **Exam Extra:** The 1982 New Delhi Asian Games marked the widespread introduction of colour television broadcasting in India.

### Q617 · Asian Games & Commonwealth Games

In which year and city were the inaugural British Empire Games (now known as the Commonwealth Games) held?

A. 1930, Hamilton
B. 1934, London
C. 1938, Sydney
D. 1950, Auckland

**Answer:** A. 1930, Hamilton
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 1930, Hamilton (Correct):** The inaugural British Empire Games—now known as the Commonwealth Games—were held in 1930 in Hamilton, Ontario, Canada, featuring 11 countries and 6 sports.
- **B — 1934, London:** Hosted the 2nd edition, marking India's Commonwealth Games debut.
- **C — 1938, Sydney:** Hosted the 3rd edition, the first Games staged in the Southern Hemisphere.
- **D — 1950, Auckland:** Hosted the 4th edition following a 12-year gap caused by World War II.
- **Exam Extra:** India did not participate in the 1930 inaugural edition; its debut came at the 1934 London Games, where Rashid Anwar won a Wrestling Bronze.

### Q618 · Asian Games & Commonwealth Games

Who won India's first Commonwealth Games Gold medal in athletics at the 1958 Cardiff Games?

A. Rashid Anwar
B. Milkha Singh
C. K. D. Jadhav
D. P. T. Usha

**Answer:** B. Milkha Singh
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — Milkha Singh (Correct):** Milkha Singh won Gold in the Men's 440 yards (400m) race at the 1958 Cardiff Commonwealth Games, capturing India's first Commonwealth Games Gold medal in athletics.
- **A — Rashid Anwar:** Won India's first-ever Commonwealth Games medal (Bronze in Wrestling) at the 1934 London Games.
- **C — K. D. Jadhav:** Won Independent India's first individual Olympic medal (Bronze in Wrestling) at 1952 Helsinki.
- **D — P. T. Usha:** Won multiple Asian Games Golds in 1986, but never won a Commonwealth Games Gold medal.
- **Exam Extra:** Commonwealth Sport (formerly the Commonwealth Games Federation), headquartered in London, governs the Commonwealth Games.

### Q619 · Asian Games & Commonwealth Games

At which edition of the Commonwealth Games did India achieve its all-time best tally of 101 medals?

A. 2002 Manchester
B. 2006 Melbourne
C. 2010 New Delhi
D. 2018 Gold Coast

**Answer:** C. 2010 New Delhi
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 2010 New Delhi (Correct):** India achieved its all-time best Commonwealth Games tally of 101 medals (38 Gold, 27 Silver, 36 Bronze) and finished 2nd overall when hosting the 19th edition in New Delhi in 2010.
- **A — 2002 Manchester:** India finished 4th in the standings with 69 medals (30 Gold, 22 Silver, 17 Bronze).
- **B — 2006 Melbourne:** India finished 4th with 50 medals (22 Gold, 17 Silver, 11 Bronze).
- **D — 2018 Gold Coast:** India finished 3rd overall with 66 medals (26 Gold, 20 Silver, 20 Bronze).
- **Exam Extra:** At the 2022 Birmingham CWG, India won 61 medals (4th place), highlighted by a historic Gold for the Women's Fours Lawn Bowls team and Silver in Women's T20 Cricket's CWG debut.

### Q620 · Cricket, Football, Tennis, Badminton & Chess

How many players from one team are on the field in a standard football match?

A. 7
B. 9
C. 11
D. 15

**Answer:** C. 11
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 11 (Correct):** In a standard football (soccer) match governed by FIFA Laws of the Game, each team has 11 players on the field, consisting of one goalkeeper and ten outfield players.
- **A — 7:** Active players per side on the court/pool in Kabaddi, Water Polo, Netball, and Handball.
- **B — 9:** Players per side on the field in Baseball and Kho-Kho.
- **D — 15:** Players per side on the field in Rugby Union (while Rugby League fields 13).
- **Exam Extra:** Football's international governing body, FIFA, was founded on 21 May 1904 in Paris and is headquartered in Zurich, Switzerland.

### Q621 · Cricket, Football, Tennis, Badminton & Chess

What is the standard length of a cricket pitch from stump to stump?

A. 18 yards
B. 20 yards
C. 22 yards
D. 24 yards

**Answer:** C. 22 yards
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 22 yards (Correct):** A standard cricket pitch measures 22 yards (66 feet or 20.12 metres) in length from stump to stump and 10 feet (3.05 metres) in width.
- **A — 18 yards:** Shorter than a cricket pitch; 18 m x 9 m are the standard dimensions of a Volleyball court.
- **B — 20 yards:** Equals 60 feet, close to the 60 feet 6 inches distance from pitcher's mound to home plate in Baseball.
- **D — 24 yards:** Longer than regulation; 24 m is the exact distance between the two poles on a Kho-Kho court.
- **Exam Extra:** Standard cricket stumps stand 28 inches (71.1 cm) above the ground, with the three stumps spanning a total width of 9 inches (22.86 cm).

### Q622 · Cricket, Football, Tennis, Badminton & Chess

Which team did India defeat in the final at Bridgetown, Barbados to win the 2024 ICC Men's T20 World Cup?

A. Australia
B. England
C. New Zealand
D. South Africa

**Answer:** D. South Africa
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — South Africa (Correct):** India defeated South Africa by 7 runs in the final at Kensington Oval in Bridgetown, Barbados on 29 June 2024 under Rohit Sharma's captaincy to win its second ICC Men's T20 World Cup.
- **A — Australia:** Defeated India by 6 wickets in the 2023 ICC Men's ODI World Cup final at Ahmedabad.
- **B — England:** Defeated by India in the 2024 T20 World Cup semi-final and won the 2022 T20 World Cup in Australia.
- **C — New Zealand:** Finished runner-up to India in the 2025 ICC Champions Trophy and the 2026 Men's T20 World Cup.
- **Exam Extra:** Jasprit Bumrah was named Player of the Tournament in the 2024 T20 World Cup, and India later won a record third title in 2026 under Suryakumar Yadav.

### Q623 · Cricket, Football, Tennis, Badminton & Chess

Which country has won the FIFA Men's World Cup a record five times?

A. Brazil
B. Germany
C. Italy
D. Argentina

**Answer:** A. Brazil
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — Brazil (Correct):** Brazil is the most successful nation in FIFA Men's World Cup history, having won a record five titles (1958, 1962, 1970, 1994, and 2002).
- **B — Germany:** Has won 4 FIFA Men's World Cup titles (1954, 1974, 1990, and 2014).
- **C — Italy:** Has won 4 FIFA Men's World Cup titles (1934, 1938, 1982, and 2006).
- **D — Argentina:** Has won 3 FIFA Men's World Cup titles (1978, 1986, and 2022).
- **Exam Extra:** Uruguay won the inaugural FIFA World Cup in 1930 by defeating Argentina 4-2 in Montevideo.

### Q624 · Cricket, Football, Tennis, Badminton & Chess

Which country won the 2022 FIFA Men's World Cup in Qatar by defeating France on penalties?

A. Spain
B. Argentina
C. Croatia
D. Morocco

**Answer:** B. Argentina
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — Argentina (Correct):** Argentina won the 2022 FIFA Men's World Cup in Qatar, defeating defending champions France 4-2 on penalties after a 3-3 draw in the final.
- **A — Spain:** Won the 2010 FIFA World Cup, the 2024 UEFA Euro (record 4th Euro title), and the 2026 FIFA World Cup.
- **C — Croatia:** Finished runners-up in 2018 and secured 3rd place at the 2022 FIFA World Cup.
- **D — Morocco:** Finished 4th at Qatar 2022, becoming the first African nation ever to reach a FIFA World Cup semi-final.
- **Exam Extra:** The official mascot of the 2022 Qatar FIFA World Cup was 'La'eeb', and Lionel Messi won the Golden Ball as the tournament's best player.

### Q625 · Cricket, Football, Tennis, Badminton & Chess

Which tennis Grand Slam tournament is played on grass courts?

A. Australian Open
B. French Open
C. US Open
D. Wimbledon

**Answer:** D. Wimbledon
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — Wimbledon (Correct):** Wimbledon (The Championships), played at the All England Lawn Tennis and Croquet Club in London since 1877, is the only Grand Slam tournament played on grass courts.
- **A — Australian Open:** Played on hard courts (GreenSet) every January at Melbourne Park since shifting from grass in 1988.
- **B — French Open:** Played on red clay courts at Stade Roland Garros in Paris during May-June; Rafael Nadal won it a record 14 times.
- **C — US Open:** Played on hard courts (Laykold) at Flushing Meadows, New York during August-September.
- **Exam Extra:** Wimbledon (est. 1877) is the oldest tennis Grand Slam tournament; Roger Federer holds the men's singles record there with 8 titles.

### Q626 · Cricket, Football, Tennis, Badminton & Chess

Who was the first Indian player to win a Tennis Grand Slam title?

A. Leander Paes
B. Mahesh Bhupathi
C. Vijay Amritraj
D. Rohan Bopanna

**Answer:** B. Mahesh Bhupathi
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — Mahesh Bhupathi (Correct):** Mahesh Bhupathi became the first Indian player to win a Tennis Grand Slam title when he won the 1997 French Open Mixed Doubles crown with Rika Hiraki.
- **A — Leander Paes:** India's most decorated Grand Slam winner (18 titles), winning his first Slam at the 1999 French Open Men's Doubles.
- **C — Vijay Amritraj:** Reached two Wimbledon and two US Open Men's Singles quarter-finals, but never won a Grand Slam title.
- **D — Rohan Bopanna:** Became the oldest player (age 43) to win a Men's Doubles Grand Slam title at the 2024 Australian Open.
- **Exam Extra:** Together, Leander Paes (18), Mahesh Bhupathi (12), Sania Mirza (6), and Rohan Bopanna (2) have won 38 Grand Slam titles for India.

### Q627 · Cricket, Football, Tennis, Badminton & Chess

Who is the only tennis player in history to complete the Calendar 'Golden Slam' (all four Grand Slams plus Olympic Singles Gold in 1988)?

A. Steffi Graf
B. Serena Williams
C. Margaret Court
D. Martina Navratilova

**Answer:** A. Steffi Graf
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — Steffi Graf (Correct):** German tennis legend Steffi Graf achieved the only 'Golden Slam' in tennis history in 1988 by winning all four Grand Slam singles titles and the Seoul Olympic Gold medal in the same calendar year.
- **B — Serena Williams:** Won 23 Open Era Grand Slam singles titles and the 2012 Olympic Singles Gold, completing a Career Golden Slam across multiple years.
- **C — Margaret Court:** Holds the all-time overall record of 24 Grand Slam singles titles and won a Calendar Grand Slam in 1970, when tennis was not in the Olympics.
- **D — Martina Navratilova:** Won 18 Grand Slam singles titles and 167 WTA singles titles overall, but never won Olympic Singles Gold.
- **Exam Extra:** Novak Djokovic holds the Open Era and men's record of 24 Grand Slam singles titles and is the only man to achieve a Triple Career Grand Slam.

### Q628 · Cricket, Football, Tennis, Badminton & Chess

Who became the first Indian shuttler to win the prestigious All England Open Badminton Championship in 1980?

A. Pullela Gopichand
B. Syed Modi
C. Nandu Natekar
D. Prakash Padukone

**Answer:** D. Prakash Padukone
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — Prakash Padukone (Correct):** Prakash Padukone won the Men's Singles title at the prestigious All England Open Badminton Championship in 1980, defeating Liem Swie King in the final to become the first Indian champion.
- **A — Pullela Gopichand:** Became the second Indian shuttler to win the All England Open Men's Singles title in 2001.
- **B — Syed Modi:** Eight-time Indian National Badminton Champion and 1982 Commonwealth Games Men's Singles Gold medalist.
- **C — Nandu Natekar:** Became the first Indian to win an international badminton title abroad (1956 Selangor International), though not the All England Open.
- **Exam Extra:** Badminton is governed globally by the Badminton World Federation (BWF), headquartered in Kuala Lumpur, Malaysia.

### Q629 · Cricket, Football, Tennis, Badminton & Chess

Who was India's first Chess Grandmaster, earning the title in 1988?

A. Viswanathan Anand
B. Dibyendu Barua
C. Pravin Thipsay
D. Pentala Harikrishna

**Answer:** A. Viswanathan Anand
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — Viswanathan Anand (Correct):** Viswanathan Anand became India's first Chess Grandmaster in 1988 and went on to win five World Chess Championships.
- **B — Dibyendu Barua:** Became India's second Chess Grandmaster in 1991.
- **C — Pravin Thipsay:** Became India's third Chess Grandmaster in 1997.
- **D — Pentala Harikrishna:** Became India's youngest Chess Grandmaster at the time in 2001 at age 15.
- **Exam Extra:** Viswanathan Anand was also the first recipient of India's highest sporting honour, the Rajiv Gandhi Khel Ratna (now Major Dhyan Chand Khel Ratna) Award, in 1991-92.

### Q630 · Cricket, Football, Tennis, Badminton & Chess

Who became the youngest undisputed World Chess Champion in history in December 2024 by defeating Ding Liren?

A. R. Praggnanandhaa
B. Arjun Erigaisi
C. Gukesh D
D. Vidit Gujrathi

**Answer:** C. Gukesh D
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — Gukesh D (Correct):** Gukesh D defeated defending champion Ding Liren 7.5-6.5 in Singapore on 12 December 2024 at age 18 years and 195 days to become the youngest undisputed World Chess Champion in history.
- **A — R. Praggnanandhaa:** Indian Grandmaster who finished runner-up at the 2023 FIDE World Cup against Magnus Carlsen.
- **B — Arjun Erigaisi:** Indian Grandmaster who crossed the 2800 Elo rating mark in 2024 and won team Gold at the 2024 Chess Olympiad.
- **D — Vidit Gujrathi:** Indian Grandmaster who captained India to Gold in the 2020 Online Chess Olympiad and won Open team Gold at Budapest 2024.
- **Exam Extra:** Earlier in September 2024, India won a historic Double Gold (Open and Women's sections) plus the Gaprindashvili Cup at the 45th FIDE Chess Olympiad in Budapest.

### Q631 · Cups/Trophies, Players, Terminology & Stadiums

The Thomas Cup is associated with which sport?

A. Badminton
B. Table Tennis
C. Lawn Tennis
D. Squash

**Answer:** A. Badminton
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — Badminton (Correct):** The Thomas Cup is the premier international Men's Team Championship in Badminton, in which India won its maiden title in 2022 by defeating 14-time champions Indonesia 3-0 in Bangkok.
- **B — Table Tennis:** International team championships are the Swaythling Cup (Men) and Corbillon Cup (Women).
- **C — Lawn Tennis:** International team championships are the Davis Cup (Men) and Billie Jean King Cup (Women).
- **D — Squash:** International team play is contested at the World Team Squash Championships rather than the Thomas Cup.
- **Exam Extra:** In international Badminton, the Uber Cup is the Women's Team Championship and the Sudirman Cup is the Mixed Team Championship.

### Q632 · Cups/Trophies, Players, Terminology & Stadiums

The Durand Cup, established in 1888 in Shimla as Asia's oldest tournament in its discipline, is associated with which sport?

A. Field Hockey
B. Cricket
C. Polo
D. Football

**Answer:** D. Football
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — Football (Correct):** The Durand Cup, established in 1888 in Shimla, is a premier Football tournament—the oldest football competition in Asia and the third oldest in the world.
- **A — Field Hockey:** Oldest Indian tournaments include the Beighton Cup (est. 1895, Kolkata) and the Aga Khan Cup (Mumbai).
- **B — Cricket:** Historic Indian domestic trophies include the Ranji Trophy (est. 1934), Duleep Trophy, and Irani Cup.
- **C — Polo:** Historic Indian polo tournaments include the Ezra Cup (est. 1880, Kolkata) and Radha Mohan Cup.
- **Exam Extra:** Another major Indian domestic football competition is the Santosh Trophy (est. 1941), the inter-state Senior National Football Championship.

### Q633 · Cups/Trophies, Players, Terminology & Stadiums

The Swaythling Cup and Corbillon Cup are international team trophies awarded in which sport?

A. Badminton
B. Volleyball
C. Table Tennis
D. Basketball

**Answer:** C. Table Tennis
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — Table Tennis (Correct):** The Swaythling Cup (Men's Team) and Corbillon Cup (Women's Team) are awarded at the World Team Table Tennis Championships.
- **A — Badminton:** Awards the Thomas Cup (Men's Team), Uber Cup (Women's Team), and Sudirman Cup (Mixed Team).
- **B — Volleyball:** Domestic Indian competitions include the Centennial Cup and Federation Cup.
- **D — Basketball:** Major tournaments include the Todd Memorial Trophy (Indian Men's National) and the William Jones Cup.
- **Exam Extra:** In Indian domestic Table Tennis, the Barna-Bellack Cup (Men), Jayalakshmi Cup (Women), and Rajkumari Challenge Cup (Junior) are major trophies.

### Q634 · Cups/Trophies, Players, Terminology & Stadiums

The Ezra Cup, started in 1880 in Kolkata, is associated with which sport?

A. Golf
B. Rowing
C. Billiards
D. Polo

**Answer:** D. Polo
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — Polo (Correct):** The Ezra Cup, founded in 1880 in Kolkata by Sir David Ezra, is the world's oldest Polo tournament.
- **A — Golf:** Major international team trophies include the Ryder Cup (Men's Europe vs USA) and Solheim Cup (Women's Europe vs USA).
- **B — Rowing:** The Wellington Trophy is India's premier domestic rowing trophy.
- **C — Billiards:** The Arthur Walker Trophy is associated with billiards in India.
- **Exam Extra:** Other notable Indian Polo trophies include the Radha Mohan Cup, Prithi Singh Cup, and Winchester Cup; a standard polo team has 4 players per side.

### Q635 · Cups/Trophies, Players, Terminology & Stadiums

Which Indian sports trophy is awarded annually to the overall top-performing university in inter-university competitions?

A. Maulana Abul Kalam Azad (MAKA) Trophy
B. Raja Bhalindra Singh Trophy
C. Dhyan Chand Trophy
D. C. K. Nayudu Trophy

**Answer:** A. Maulana Abul Kalam Azad (MAKA) Trophy
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — Maulana Abul Kalam Azad (MAKA) Trophy (Correct):** The Maulana Abul Kalam Azad (MAKA) Trophy, instituted in 1956-57, is awarded annually to the overall top-performing university in inter-university tournaments in India.
- **B — Raja Bhalindra Singh Trophy:** Awarded to the overall champion team with the most Gold medals at the National Games of India.
- **C — Dhyan Chand Trophy:** A prestigious domestic Field Hockey tournament in India.
- **D — C. K. Nayudu Trophy:** A domestic Indian U-23/U-25 First-Class Cricket championship.
- **Exam Extra:** The trophy is named after Maulana Abul Kalam Azad, India's first Union Minister of Education, whose birth anniversary (November 11) is National Education Day.

### Q636 · Cups/Trophies, Players, Terminology & Stadiums

Which Indian sprinter is popularly nicknamed the 'Dhing Express'?

A. P. T. Usha
B. Dutee Chand
C. Swapna Barman
D. Hima Das

**Answer:** D. Hima Das
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — Hima Das (Correct):** Assam sprinter Hima Das, who hails from Dhing village in Nagaon district, is popularly nicknamed the 'Dhing Express'.
- **A — P. T. Usha:** Nicknamed the 'Payyoli Express' and 'Golden Girl'; elected first woman President of the IOA in December 2022.
- **B — Dutee Chand:** Former Indian national 100m record holder and double Silver medalist at the 2018 Asian Games.
- **C — Swapna Barman:** Heptathlete from West Bengal who won India's first Asian Games Gold medal in Heptathlon in 2018.
- **Exam Extra:** In 2018, Hima Das became the first Indian athlete to win a Gold medal in a track event (400m) at the IAAF World U-20 Championships.

### Q637 · Cups/Trophies, Players, Terminology & Stadiums

The terms 'Birdie', 'Eagle', 'Bogey', and 'Tee' are associated with which sport?

A. Polo
B. Golf
C. Snooker
D. Archery

**Answer:** B. Golf
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — Golf (Correct):** The terms 'Birdie' (1 under par), 'Eagle' (2 under par), 'Bogey' (1 over par), and 'Tee' (starting area) are standard terms used in Golf.
- **A — Polo:** Uses terms such as Chukker (a 7.5-minute playing period), Mallet, Handicap, and Ride-off.
- **C — Snooker:** Uses terms such as Cue, Break, Pot, Cannon, Frame, and Baulk Line.
- **D — Archery:** Uses terms such as Bull's Eye, Quiver, Recurve, and Compound.
- **Exam Extra:** An 'Albatross' (also called a Double Eagle) in Golf refers to scoring 3 strokes under par on a single hole.

### Q638 · Cups/Trophies, Players, Terminology & Stadiums

'En Passant', 'Castling', and 'Zugzwang' are technical terms used in which game?

A. Chess
B. Bridge
C. Billiards
D. Fencing

**Answer:** A. Chess
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — Chess (Correct):** 'En Passant' (special pawn capture), 'Castling' (king-rook move), and 'Zugzwang' (a position where any legal move worsens a player's situation) are technical terms in Chess.
- **B — Bridge:** Card game using terms such as Trump, Dummy, Slam, Rubber, and Vulnerability.
- **C — Billiards:** Cue sport using terms such as Cue, Hazard, Jigger, and Cannon.
- **D — Fencing:** Combat sport using terms such as Foil, Epee, Sabre, Parry, and Riposte.
- **Exam Extra:** International Chess Day is celebrated on July 20 every year to mark the founding of FIDE (International Chess Federation) on 20 July 1924 in Paris.

### Q639 · Cups/Trophies, Players, Terminology & Stadiums

In which sport are the terms 'Bully', 'Scoop', and 'Penalty Corner' used?

A. Football
B. Water Polo
C. Field Hockey
D. Handball

**Answer:** C. Field Hockey
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — Field Hockey (Correct):** 'Bully' (a method to restart play), 'Scoop' (an aerial flick of the ball), and 'Penalty Corner' are standard terms in Field Hockey.
- **A — Football:** Uses terms such as Offside, Throw-in, Corner Kick, Nutmeg, and Bicycle Kick.
- **B — Water Polo:** Uses terms such as Eggbeater kick, Exclusion foul, and Two-metre line.
- **D — Handball:** Uses terms such as Goal crease, Seven-metre throw, and Jump shot.
- **Exam Extra:** A standard Field Hockey pitch measures 91.4 m x 55 m (100 yards x 60 yards), with a shooting circle radius of 14.63 m.

### Q640 · Cups/Trophies, Players, Terminology & Stadiums

Where is the Birsa Munda International Hockey Stadium, the world's largest all-seater field hockey stadium, located?

A. Bhubaneswar
B. Rourkela
C. Ranchi
D. Jamshedpur

**Answer:** B. Rourkela
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — Rourkela (Correct):** The Birsa Munda International Hockey Stadium, recognized as the world's largest all-seater field hockey stadium, is located in Rourkela, Odisha.
- **A — Bhubaneswar:** Hosts the Kalinga Stadium, which co-hosted the 2018 and 2023 Men's Hockey World Cups.
- **C — Ranchi:** Home to the Marang Gomke Jaipal Singh AstroTurf Hockey Stadium and the JSCA International Cricket Stadium.
- **D — Jamshedpur:** Home to the JRD Tata Sports Complex (football and athletics) and Keenan Stadium.
- **Exam Extra:** Built in 2023 with a seating capacity of over 20,000, it co-hosted the 2023 FIH Men's Hockey World Cup alongside Kalinga Stadium in Bhubaneswar.

### Q641 · Cups/Trophies, Players, Terminology & Stadiums

Which Indian cricket stadium is recognized as the world's first solar-powered cricket stadium?

A. Eden Gardens, Kolkata
B. Wankhede Stadium, Mumbai
C. Narendra Modi Stadium, Ahmedabad
D. M. Chinnaswamy Stadium, Bengaluru

**Answer:** D. M. Chinnaswamy Stadium, Bengaluru
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — M. Chinnaswamy Stadium, Bengaluru (Correct):** M. Chinnaswamy Stadium in Bengaluru became the world's first solar-powered cricket stadium after installing a 400-kW rooftop solar power plant in 2015.
- **A — Eden Gardens, Kolkata:** Established in 1864, it is India's oldest and second-largest cricket stadium.
- **B — Wankhede Stadium, Mumbai:** Hosted the 2011 ICC Men's ODI World Cup Final and houses the BCCI headquarters.
- **C — Narendra Modi Stadium, Ahmedabad:** The world's largest cricket stadium by seating capacity and venue of the 2023 ODI World Cup Final.
- **Exam Extra:** The Narendra Modi Stadium in Ahmedabad is the world's largest cricket stadium with a capacity of around 1,32,000 spectators.

### Q642 · Important National & International Days (Jan-Dec)

Pravasi Bharatiya Divas (NRI Day) is celebrated on January 9 to commemorate which historical event?

A. Mahatma Gandhi's return from South Africa
B. Swami Vivekananda's Chicago speech
C. Foundation of the Indian National Congress
D. Adoption of Purna Swaraj resolution

**Answer:** A. Mahatma Gandhi's return from South Africa
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — Mahatma Gandhi's return from South Africa (Correct):** Pravasi Bharatiya Divas (NRI Day) is observed on January 9 to mark Mahatma Gandhi's return to India from South Africa on 9 January 1915.
- **B — Swami Vivekananda's Chicago speech:** Delivered on 11 September 1893; his birth anniversary (January 12) is celebrated as National Youth Day.
- **C — Foundation of the Indian National Congress:** Occurred on 28 December 1885 at Gokuldas Tejpal Sanskrit College in Bombay.
- **D — Adoption of Purna Swaraj resolution:** Passed at the Lahore Session of the Congress in December 1929 and celebrated on 26 January 1930.
- **Exam Extra:** First celebrated in 2003, Pravasi Bharatiya Divas honours the contribution of the overseas Indian community to India's development.

### Q643 · Important National & International Days (Jan-Dec)

On which date is National Voters' Day observed in India to mark the foundation of the Election Commission of India?

A. January 15
B. January 24
C. January 25
D. January 26

**Answer:** C. January 25
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — January 25 (Correct):** National Voters' Day is celebrated every year on January 25 to commemorate the foundation day of the Election Commission of India (25 January 1950).
- **A — January 15:** Observed as Indian Army Day, marking Field Marshal K. M. Cariappa taking over as the first Indian Commander-in-Chief in 1949.
- **B — January 24:** Observed as National Girl Child Day in India and International Day of Education globally.
- **D — January 26:** Observed as Republic Day of India, marking the day the Constitution came into force in 1950.
- **Exam Extra:** January 25 is also observed across India as National Tourism Day to promote domestic tourism and cultural heritage.

### Q644 · Important National & International Days (Jan-Dec)
