Which CPU register holds the memory address of the next instruction to be executed?

A. Accumulator
B. Instruction Register
C. Program Counter
D. Memory Buffer Register

**Answer:** C. Program Counter
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — Program Counter (Correct):** The Program Counter (PC) holds the memory address of the next instruction to be fetched and executed by the CPU.
- **A — Accumulator:** Register that holds intermediate arithmetic and logic results produced by the ALU.
- **B — Instruction Register:** Holds the machine instruction currently being decoded and executed.
- **D — Memory Buffer Register:** Temporarily holds data being transferred to or from main memory.
- **Exam Extra:** During each fetch-decode-execute cycle, the Program Counter increments automatically to point to the following instruction unless altered by a branch or jump.

### Q725 · Computer Generations, CPU, Memory & Hardware I/O

What is the binary equivalent of decimal 8?

A. 0100
B. 1000
C. 1010
D. 1100

**Answer:** B. 1000
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 1000 (Correct):** In binary (base-2), decimal 8 equals 1×2³ + 0×2² + 0×2¹ + 0×2⁰, which is written as 1000.
- **A — 0100:** Binary representation of decimal 4 (0×8 + 1×4 + 0×2 + 0×1).
- **C — 1010:** Binary representation of decimal 10 (1×8 + 0×4 + 1×2 + 0×1).
- **D — 1100:** Binary representation of decimal 12 (1×8 + 1×4 + 0×2 + 0×1).
- **Exam Extra:** Dividing 8 repeatedly by 2 produces remainders 0, 0, 0, and 1 from bottom to top, giving 1000₂.

### Q726 · Computer Generations, CPU, Memory & Hardware I/O

How many bytes make 1 Kilobyte (KB) in traditional binary memory measurement?

A. 512 bytes
B. 1000 bytes
C. 1024 bytes
D. 2048 bytes

**Answer:** C. 1024 bytes
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 1024 bytes (Correct):** In traditional binary memory measurement, 1 Kilobyte (KB) equals 2¹⁰ = 1024 bytes.
- **A — 512 bytes:** Legacy sector size on magnetic hard disks, equal to 2⁹ bytes.
- **B — 1000 bytes:** SI decimal kilobyte (10³ bytes), often used by storage drive manufacturers.
- **D — 2048 bytes:** Equal to 2 Kilobytes in binary measurement (2¹¹ bytes).
- **Exam Extra:** To distinguish powers of two from decimal powers of ten, the IEC calls 1024 bytes 1 kibibyte (1 KiB).

### Q727 · Computer Generations, CPU, Memory & Hardware I/O

Which of the following is exclusively an output device?

A. Optical Mark Reader
B. Joystick
C. Trackball
D. Plotter

**Answer:** D. Plotter
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — Plotter (Correct):** A plotter is exclusively an output device used to print large-format vector graphics, blueprints, and engineering drawings.
- **A — Optical Mark Reader:** Input device that scans pencil or pen marks on standardized OMR exam sheets.
- **B — Joystick:** Input device used to transmit directional movement in simulations, games, and machinery control.
- **C — Trackball:** Pointing input device operated by rolling an exposed ball with the fingers or thumb.
- **Exam Extra:** Unlike raster inkjet/laser printers, traditional pen plotters draw continuous vector lines directly onto large paper sheets.

### Q728 · Computer Generations, CPU, Memory & Hardware I/O

Which technology uses magnetized ink characters on bank cheques for fast, secure automated clearing?

A. OCR
B. OMR
C. MICR
D. QR Scanner

**Answer:** C. MICR
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — MICR (Correct):** MICR (Magnetic Ink Character Recognition) reads characters printed with iron-oxide magnetic ink at the bottom of bank cheques.
- **A — OCR:** Optical Character Recognition; converts scanned images of printed or handwritten text into editable digital text.
- **B — OMR:** Optical Mark Recognition; detects marked bubbles or checkboxes on exam and survey forms.
- **D — QR Scanner:** Optical imaging reader that decodes two-dimensional Quick Response matrix barcodes.
- **Exam Extra:** Every Indian bank cheque leaf carries a 9-digit MICR code identifying the city (first 3 digits), bank (next 3), and branch (last 3).

### Q729 · Computer Generations, CPU, Memory & Hardware I/O

Which firmware program stored on the motherboard initializes and tests hardware components when a computer boots up?

A. Compiler
B. Device Driver
C. BIOS
D. Linker

**Answer:** C. BIOS
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — BIOS (Correct):** BIOS (Basic Input/Output System) is low-level firmware stored in non-volatile ROM/flash that initializes and tests hardware at startup.
- **A — Compiler:** Translator program that converts an entire high-level source file into machine code.
- **B — Device Driver:** Operating system module that enables software to communicate with a specific hardware peripheral.
- **D — Linker:** Utility that combines compiled object files and libraries into a single executable binary.
- **Exam Extra:** On power-on, the BIOS runs the Power-On Self-Test (POST) before handing control to the OS bootloader; modern systems largely use UEFI.

### Q730 · Computer Generations, CPU, Memory & Hardware I/O

Which internal computer bus carries only the memory location where data is to be read or written?

A. Data bus
B. Address bus
C. Control bus
D. Expansion bus

**Answer:** B. Address bus
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — Address bus (Correct):** The Address bus carries only the memory or I/O addresses specified by the CPU for reading or writing data.
- **A — Data bus:** Bidirectional bus that transfers actual data bits between CPU, memory, and I/O devices.
- **C — Control bus:** Carries timing and command signals such as Memory Read, Memory Write, Clock, and Interrupts.
- **D — Expansion bus:** External or peripheral bus (such as PCIe) connecting add-on cards to the motherboard.
- **Exam Extra:** The Address bus is unidirectional from CPU to memory, and an n-bit address bus can directly address up to 2ⁿ memory locations.

### Q731 · OS, DBMS, Networking, OSI 7 Layers, Ports & Cybersecurity

Which protocol is commonly used to transfer web pages securely?

A. FTP
B. SMTP
C. HTTP
D. HTTPS

**Answer:** D. HTTPS
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — HTTPS (Correct):** HTTPS (Hypertext Transfer Protocol Secure) encrypts web traffic using TLS/SSL to transfer web pages securely.
- **A — FTP:** File Transfer Protocol; transfers files between client and server in plain text without built-in encryption.
- **B — SMTP:** Simple Mail Transfer Protocol; used for sending outgoing email messages across mail servers.
- **C — HTTP:** Hypertext Transfer Protocol; transfers web pages in unencrypted plain text.
- **Exam Extra:** HTTPS operates by default on TCP port 443, whereas unencrypted HTTP operates on port 80.

### Q732 · OS, DBMS, Networking, OSI 7 Layers, Ports & Cybersecurity

In the OSI model, which layer is responsible for logical IP addressing and routing packets across networks?

A. Data Link Layer
B. Network Layer
C. Transport Layer
D. Session Layer

**Answer:** B. Network Layer
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — Network Layer (Correct):** The Network Layer (Layer 3) of the OSI model handles logical IP addressing, packet forwarding, and routing across networks.
- **A — Data Link Layer:** Layer 2; handles MAC physical addressing, framing, and error detection on a local link.
- **C — Transport Layer:** Layer 4; manages end-to-end delivery, segmentation, and port addressing via TCP or UDP.
- **D — Session Layer:** Layer 5; establishes, synchronizes, and terminates communication sessions between applications.
- **Exam Extra:** Routers operate at Layer 3 (Network Layer), whereas network switches primarily operate at Layer 2 (Data Link Layer).

### Q733 · OS, DBMS, Networking, OSI 7 Layers, Ports & Cybersecurity

Which layer of the OSI model sits immediately above the Physical Layer (Layer 1)?

A. Data Link Layer
B. Network Layer
C. Transport Layer
D. Presentation Layer

**Answer:** A. Data Link Layer
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — Data Link Layer (Correct):** The Data Link Layer (Layer 2) sits directly above the Physical Layer (Layer 1) in the 7-layer OSI reference model.
- **B — Network Layer:** Layer 3 of the OSI model, positioned above the Data Link Layer.
- **C — Transport Layer:** Layer 4 of the OSI model, managing reliable or unreliable end-to-end transport.
- **D — Presentation Layer:** Layer 6 of the OSI model, responsible for data formatting, compression, and encryption.
- **Exam Extra:** From Layer 1 to Layer 7, the OSI layers are Physical, Data Link, Network, Transport, Session, Presentation, and Application.

### Q734 · OS, DBMS, Networking, OSI 7 Layers, Ports & Cybersecurity

Which default TCP port number is assigned to SSH (Secure Shell) for encrypted remote login?

A. Port 21
B. Port 23
C. Port 25
D. Port 22

**Answer:** D. Port 22
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — Port 22 (Correct):** SSH (Secure Shell) uses TCP port 22 by default to provide encrypted remote terminal access and secure file transfers.
- **A — Port 21:** Default control port for FTP (File Transfer Protocol).
- **B — Port 23:** Default port for Telnet, an unencrypted remote command-line protocol.
- **C — Port 25:** Default port for SMTP (Simple Mail Transfer Protocol) for mail server routing.
- **Exam Extra:** SSH replaced Telnet (port 23) for remote server administration because SSH encrypts login credentials and session data.

### Q735 · OS, DBMS, Networking, OSI 7 Layers, Ports & Cybersecurity

Which network device operates at Layer 1 (Physical Layer) and broadcasts incoming signals to all ports?

A. Hub
B. Switch
C. Router
D. Gateway

**Answer:** A. Hub
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — Hub (Correct):** A Hub is a Layer 1 (Physical Layer) device that repeats and broadcasts every incoming electrical signal out to all other ports.
- **B — Switch:** Layer 2 device that inspects MAC addresses and forwards frames only to the intended destination port.
- **C — Router:** Layer 3 device that forwards IP packets between separate networks using routing tables.
- **D — Gateway:** Network node that connects and translates between two dissimilar network architectures or protocols.
- **Exam Extra:** Because a hub broadcasts every frame to all connected ports, all devices attached to a hub share a single collision domain.

### Q736 · OS, DBMS, Networking, OSI 7 Layers, Ports & Cybersecurity

In a Relational Database Management System (RDBMS), what is the primary purpose of a Foreign Key?

A. Uniquely identifies each row within its own table
B. Links a column in one table to the Primary Key of another table
C. Stores encrypted passwords for database users
D. Automatically removes duplicate rows from query output

**Answer:** B. Links a column in one table to the Primary Key of another table
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — Links a column in one table to the Primary Key of another table (Correct):** In an RDBMS, a Foreign Key links a column (or set of columns) in one table to the Primary Key of another table to maintain referential integrity.
- **A — Uniquely identifies each row within its own table:** Role of a Primary Key, which must be unique and cannot be NULL.
- **C — Stores encrypted passwords for database users:** Handled by database authentication and access-control mechanisms, not a foreign key.
- **D — Automatically removes duplicate rows from query output:** Achieved using the SQL SELECT DISTINCT keyword rather than keys.
- **Exam Extra:** Referential integrity ensures a foreign key value must either match an existing primary key in the referenced parent table or be NULL.

### Q737 · OS, DBMS, Networking, OSI 7 Layers, Ports & Cybersecurity

Which SQL command belongs to Data Definition Language (DDL) rather than Data Manipulation Language (DML)?

A. INSERT
B. UPDATE
C. DELETE
D. CREATE

**Answer:** D. CREATE
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — CREATE (Correct):** CREATE is a Data Definition Language (DDL) command used to define new database objects such as tables, views, or schemas.
- **A — INSERT:** Data Manipulation Language (DML) command used to add new records into a table.
- **B — UPDATE:** DML command used to modify values in existing table records.
- **C — DELETE:** DML command used to remove specific rows from a table.
- **Exam Extra:** Common SQL DDL commands include CREATE, ALTER, DROP, and TRUNCATE, which modify database structure rather than individual rows.

### Q738 · OS, DBMS, Networking, OSI 7 Layers, Ports & Cybersecurity

Which core component of an operating system directly manages CPU scheduling, memory allocation, and hardware communication?

A. Shell
B. File Explorer
C. Kernel
D. Task Scheduler

**Answer:** C. Kernel
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — Kernel (Correct):** The Kernel is the core component of an operating system that directly manages CPU scheduling, memory allocation, and hardware I/O.
- **A — Shell:** User-facing command interpreter or GUI layer that passes user commands to the operating system kernel.
- **B — File Explorer:** Graphical file manager application used to browse directories and manage files.
- **D — Task Scheduler:** Utility program that launches predefined scripts or tasks at scheduled times or triggers.
- **Exam Extra:** Linux, created by Linus Torvalds in 1991, is an open-source kernel that powers Android smartphones as well as most cloud servers.

### Q739 · OS, DBMS, Networking, OSI 7 Layers, Ports & Cybersecurity

Which type of malware disguises itself as legitimate software to trick users into installing it without self-replicating?

A. Worm
B. Ransomware
C. Spyware
D. Trojan horse

**Answer:** D. Trojan horse
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — Trojan horse (Correct):** A Trojan horse disguises itself as useful or legitimate software to trick users into running it, without self-replicating like a worm or virus.
- **A — Worm:** Standalone self-replicating malware that spreads automatically across networks without user intervention.
- **B — Ransomware:** Malware that encrypts files or locks a system and demands a ransom payment for restoration.
- **C — Spyware:** Covert malware designed to monitor user activity, log keystrokes, or steal confidential data.
- **Exam Extra:** Named after the wooden horse of Greek mythology, a Trojan often creates a hidden backdoor allowing remote attackers to access the system.

### Q740 · OS, DBMS, Networking, OSI 7 Layers, Ports & Cybersecurity

Which cyberattack uses deceptive emails disguised as trusted institutions to steal login credentials or banking details?

A. Phishing
B. Spoofing
C. Sniffing
D. DDoS attack

**Answer:** A. Phishing
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — Phishing (Correct):** Phishing is a social-engineering cyberattack that uses deceptive emails or messages posing as trusted organizations to steal credentials or financial data.
- **B — Spoofing:** Falsifying a sender address, IP address, MAC address, or caller ID to impersonate a trusted source.
- **C — Sniffing:** Capturing and inspecting unencrypted data packets travelling across a network.
- **D — DDoS attack:** Distributed Denial of Service; overwhelming a server with traffic from many compromised machines to disrupt availability.
- **Exam Extra:** Targeted attacks are called spear phishing, while phishing via SMS is known as smishing and voice-call phishing is called vishing.

### Q741 · OS, DBMS, Networking, OSI 7 Layers, Ports & Cybersecurity

Which network protocol automatically assigns IP addresses, subnet masks, and default gateways to client devices?

A. DNS
B. ARP
C. DHCP
D. ICMP

**Answer:** C. DHCP
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — DHCP (Correct):** DHCP (Dynamic Host Configuration Protocol) automatically assigns IP addresses, subnet masks, and default gateways to devices joining a network.
- **A — DNS:** Domain Name System; resolves human-readable domain names (like example.com) into IP addresses.
- **B — ARP:** Address Resolution Protocol; maps a known IPv4 address to a physical MAC address on a local network.
- **D — ICMP:** Internet Control Message Protocol; sends error messages and operational diagnostics used by tools like ping.
- **Exam Extra:** DHCP allocates IP addresses through a four-step exchange known as DORA: Discover, Offer, Request, and Acknowledge.

### Q742 · MS Word, MS Excel Formulas, Shortcuts & Abbreviations

In MS Excel, which symbol must begin every formula or function?

A. #
B. @
C. =
D. $

**Answer:** C. =
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — = (Correct):** In MS Excel, every formula or function must begin with an equals sign (=) so Excel evaluates it as an expression rather than plain text.
- **A — #:** Used as a prefix in Excel error values such as #DIV/0!, #VALUE!, and #REF!.
- **B — @:** Implicit intersection operator used in Excel dynamic array formulas and structured table references.
- **D — $:** Used inside cell references (e.g., $A$1) to lock a column or row as an absolute reference.
- **Exam Extra:** Pressing F4 while editing a cell reference in Excel cycles between relative (A1), absolute ($A$1), and mixed (A$1, $A1) references.

### Q743 · MS Word, MS Excel Formulas, Shortcuts & Abbreviations

What will the MS Excel formula =SUM(10, 20, 30)/COUNT(10, 20, 30) return?

A. 10
B. 20
C. 30
D. 60

**Answer:** B. 20
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 20 (Correct):** SUM(10, 20, 30) evaluates to 60 and COUNT(10, 20, 30) evaluates to 3, so 60 / 3 equals 20.
- **A — 10:** The first argument value alone, not the result of dividing the sum by the count.
- **C — 30:** The maximum argument value, which would be returned by =MAX(10, 20, 30).
- **D — 60:** The total sum returned by =SUM(10, 20, 30) before dividing by 3.
- **Exam Extra:** Dividing SUM by COUNT computes the arithmetic mean, producing the exact same result as =AVERAGE(10, 20, 30).

### Q744 · MS Word, MS Excel Formulas, Shortcuts & Abbreviations

In MS Excel, which function counts only the cells in a range that contain numeric values?

A. COUNTA
B. COUNTBLANK
C. SUMIF
D. COUNT

**Answer:** D. COUNT
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — COUNT (Correct):** The COUNT function in MS Excel counts only the cells within a specified range that contain numeric values (numbers or dates).
- **A — COUNTA:** Counts all non-empty cells in a range, including text, numbers, logical values, and errors.
- **B — COUNTBLANK:** Counts only the completely empty cells within a specified range.
- **C — SUMIF:** Sums the numeric values in cells that satisfy a given condition.
- **Exam Extra:** To count cells matching a specific condition (such as '>50'), Excel provides the COUNTIF(range, criteria) function.

### Q745 · MS Word, MS Excel Formulas, Shortcuts & Abbreviations

In MS Word, which keyboard shortcut opens the 'Find and Replace' dialog box directly on the Replace tab?

A. Ctrl + F
B. Ctrl + G
C. Ctrl + H
D. Ctrl + R

**Answer:** C. Ctrl + H
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — Ctrl + H (Correct):** In MS Word, Ctrl + H opens the 'Find and Replace' dialog box directly on the Replace tab.
- **A — Ctrl + F:** Opens the Navigation pane (Find tool) to search for words or phrases without replacing.
- **B — Ctrl + G:** Opens the Go To tab to jump directly to a specific page, section, bookmark, or line.
- **D — Ctrl + R:** Aligns the selected paragraph text to the right margin in MS Word.
- **Exam Extra:** In MS Word, Ctrl + E centers a paragraph while Ctrl + J justifies text evenly across both margins.

### Q746 · MS Word, MS Excel Formulas, Shortcuts & Abbreviations

Which keyboard shortcut inserts a hyperlink in both MS Word and MS Excel?

A. Ctrl + K
B. Ctrl + L
C. Ctrl + M
D. Ctrl + N

**Answer:** A. Ctrl + K
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — Ctrl + K (Correct):** Ctrl + K opens the Insert Hyperlink dialog box in both MS Word and MS Excel (as well as PowerPoint).
- **B — Ctrl + L:** Left-aligns a paragraph in MS Word, or opens Create Table in MS Excel.
- **C — Ctrl + M:** Increases paragraph indent in MS Word, or inserts a new slide in MS PowerPoint.
- **D — Ctrl + N:** Creates a new blank document, workbook, or presentation across MS Office apps.
- **Exam Extra:** In MS Word, pressing Ctrl + Shift + F9 unlinks a selected hyperlink or field and converts it into plain static text.

### Q747 · MS Word, MS Excel Formulas, Shortcuts & Abbreviations

In MS Word, which key combination inserts a manual page break at the cursor position?

A. Shift + Enter
B. Ctrl + Enter
C. Alt + Enter
D. Ctrl + Shift + Enter

**Answer:** B. Ctrl + Enter
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — Ctrl + Enter (Correct):** Pressing Ctrl + Enter in MS Word inserts a manual page break at the cursor position, moving subsequent content to the top of the next page.
- **A — Shift + Enter:** Inserts a soft line break within the same paragraph without creating a new paragraph or page.
- **C — Alt + Enter:** Inserts a line break inside a single cell in MS Excel.
- **D — Ctrl + Shift + Enter:** Inserts a column break in MS Word, or enters a legacy array formula in MS Excel.
- **Exam Extra:** In MS PowerPoint, Ctrl + M inserts a new slide, whereas Ctrl + D duplicates the selected slide or object.

### Q748 · MS Word, MS Excel Formulas, Shortcuts & Abbreviations

In MS Excel, what is the maximum number of rows supported in a modern .xlsx worksheet?

A. 16,384
B. 65,536
C. 1,048,576
D. 2,097,152

**Answer:** C. 1,048,576
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 1,048,576 (Correct):** Every modern MS Excel .xlsx worksheet (Excel 2007 and later) supports up to 1,048,576 rows (2²⁰ rows).
- **A — 16,384:** Maximum number of columns (A to XFD) in a modern .xlsx worksheet, not the number of rows.
- **B — 65,536:** Maximum number of rows (2¹⁶) in legacy Excel 97–2003 .xls workbooks.
- **D — 2,097,152:** Twice the .xlsx row limit (2²¹), which is not a standard Excel worksheet limit.
- **Exam Extra:** Alongside 1,048,576 rows, an .xlsx worksheet has 16,384 columns (2¹⁴), running from column A to column XFD.

### Q749 · MS Word, MS Excel Formulas, Shortcuts & Abbreviations

What is the full form of USB in computer hardware?

A. Universal Serial Bus
B. Unified System Bus
C. Universal Storage Bridge
D. Uniform Signal Bus

**Answer:** A. Universal Serial Bus
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — Universal Serial Bus (Correct):** USB stands for Universal Serial Bus, an industry standard for cables, connectors, and serial communication protocols between computers and peripherals.
- **B — Unified System Bus:** Incorrect expansion; internal motherboard data pathways are system buses, not USB.
- **C — Universal Storage Bridge:** Incorrect expansion; USB connects keyboards, mice, printers, audio gear, and network adapters as well as storage.
- **D — Uniform Signal Bus:** Incorrect expansion; 'Serial' refers to transmitting data bits sequentially over a wire.
- **Exam Extra:** Co-developed by an Ajay Bhatt-led team in the mid-1990s, USB replaced legacy serial and parallel ports with hot-swappable Plug and Play.

### Q750 · MS Word, MS Excel Formulas, Shortcuts & Abbreviations

What does PDF stand for in digital document standards?

A. Printable Document File
B. Portable Document Format
C. Public Data File
D. Processed Document Format

**Answer:** B. Portable Document Format
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — Portable Document Format (Correct):** PDF stands for Portable Document Format, created to present documents consistently regardless of application software, hardware, or operating system.
- **A — Printable Document File:** Incorrect expansion; PDF preserves fixed formatting for both screen viewing and printing.
- **C — Public Data File:** Incorrect expansion; PDF is a document layout format, not specifically for public data.
- **D — Processed Document Format:** Incorrect expansion; 'Portable' indicates cross-platform portability across devices.
- **Exam Extra:** Developed by Adobe in 1992–1993 under John Warnock's Camelot project, PDF became an open ISO standard (ISO 32000) in 2008.
